proposed_invariants:
- No screen component defines a visible string literal; every text a person reads
  comes from the keyed front module.
- No subtask changes a refusal code or contract.json.
- The formats ($ 11.000, 3,5 g, dd/mm) and the pending mark "and N g not yet costed"
  stay byte-identical in every diff.
- No subtask rewrites a stored seeded row; only the defaults for new businesses change.
- No existing test assertion is loosened or deleted to accommodate the English text;
  expectations are translated, not weakened.
- A screen-group subtask's diff touches only the copy/ files and the ledger file it
  owns, never another group's.
- No file under web/src/screens, components or shell calls toLocale*, Intl.DateTimeFormat
  or Intl.RelativeTimeFormat; day and month names come only from copy.
- No screen, component or shell file holds a visible string literal in a variable,
  ternary, template or helper; every visible text is read from copy.
- No section's copy value outside SAME_IN_BOTH equals a Spanish value in copyBaseline.es.json,
  whatever its key.
- Every English label of a glossary concept equals that concept's `_In the app_` line
  in CONTEXT.md.
- No file outside the routes module holds a literal route path string.
- No screen-group subtask edits CONTEXT.md, the common section or a shared component.

subtasks:
  copy-move:
    id: copy-move
    title: 'S1a: move every front text into web/src/copy/ with no wording change'
    brief: "Create the keyed text package `web/src/copy/`, a pure, mechanical move.\
      \ No visible text changes.\n\n**What to build**\n- `web/src/copy/index.ts` re-exports\
      \ one section file per area. Every section exists from the start, even if empty:\
      \ `common`, `auth`, `settings`, `sales`, `customers`, `varieties`, `stock`,\
      \ `catalog`, `home`, `analysis`, `expenses`, `onboarding`, `refusals`, `format`,\
      \ `shell`, `states`. Later subtasks never edit `index.ts`.\n- Move the 16 copy\
      \ modules (`*Messages.ts` and `messages.ts` under `web/src/screens`, `components/estadosMessages.ts`,\
      \ `sales/messages.ts`) into their sections.\n  - Keep each section's `LIMITED_COPY`\
      \ and `LONG_REFUSALS`.\n  - Update `web/src/copy/copyLimits.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_MODULES`\
      \ and its glob so they read the sections.\n- Move the texts of `web/src/api/refusals.ts`\
      \ into `copy/refusals.ts`. `refusals.ts` keeps `Record<RefusalCode, Refusal>`,\
      \ exhaustive, and reads the text from there.\n- **Harvest `common`.** Every\
      \ value that appears in two or more of today's copy modules or `refusals.ts`\
      \ moves to `common`, still in Spanish, and the others import it. `messageSources.test.ts`\
      \ keeps passing, with its owners updated.\n- Type every entry `as const`. A\
      \ parametrised entry stays a function.\n- **Generate the baseline.** `web/src/architecture/fixtures/copyBaseline.es.json`\
      \ maps each key path to its Spanish value. A function entry is stored as its\
      \ output rendered with `SAMPLES`. Write the small script that generates it under\
      \ `web/scripts/`, run it once, and commit the output. The file is never hand-edited.\n\
      \n**Not in scope:** literals still inline in screens (S1b-1 ledgers them); any\
      \ wording.\n\n**Acceptance**\n- `tsc`, `oxlint`, vitest and e2e pass, with no\
      \ test edited except import paths and `copyLimits.test.ts`'s module list.\n\
      - `git grep` finds no `*Messages.ts` outside `copy/`.\n- The baseline JSON lists\
      \ every moved value.\n- A screen-rendering test still shows the same Spanish\
      \ text."
    seams:
    - web/src/copy/copyLimits.test.ts (copyProblems over each section)
    - web/src/architecture/messageSources.test.ts (findMessageCopies)
    - existing screen tests through Testing Library, unchanged expectations
    depends_on: []
    status: approved
    branch: agentic/GH-53.copy-move
    base: 0d9c626f
    reviewed_head: 248f2ed91f2c6539d16fea53bc4bb78728203577
  backend-text:
    id: backend-text
    title: 'S2: English seeds and catalog text, backend text guard, upkeep test, sample
      keeps its data'
    brief: "**Backend changes**\n- **Seeds.** `app/expenses/domain/category.py`'s\
      \ `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES` becomes (\"Growing supplies\", \"Packaging\", \"\
      Shipping and travel\", \"Other\"). `app/catalog/domain/header_footer.py` gets\
      \ the header \"\U0001F33F **CATALOG** \U0001F33F\" and the footer \"\U0001F4E9\
      \ Questions and orders by private message.\".\n  - Migrations 0003 and 0006\
      \ keep their own copies, untouched.\n  - No stored row is changed.\n- **Catalog\
      \ text.** In `app/catalog/domain/text.py`, the fixed words of a block become\
      \ English: `• N% indica / M% sativa`, `• Aroma/flavour: <aroma>`, `• Price per\
      \ gram: $X`, `• From N g: $Y each`. `NOTHING_TO_OFFER` becomes \"Nothing to\
      \ offer for now.\".\n  - `_money` and `_grams` stay byte-identical.\n  - Update\
      \ the catalog reference-set test one for one.\n  - The front preview (`telegramPreview.cases.json`)\
      \ follows: update its cases one for one.\n- **Import.** In `app/spreadsheet_import/domain/plan.py`,\
      \ the inline `\"Catálogo\"` and `\"Categoría\"` become named constants in `domain/rows.py`.\
      \ Behaviour and accepted sheet words are unchanged.\n- **Backend guard: `tests/architecture/test_app_text.py`.**\n\
      \  - It walks the AST of every string constant and f-string literal part in\
      \ `app/**/*.py` and `scripts/{drill,backup_copy,newest_backup}.py`, skipping\
      \ docstrings.\n  - It flags any hit of the marker list.\n  - Exempt paths are\
      \ closed: `app/migrations/**`, `app/spreadsheet_import/adapters/layout.py`,\
      \ `cells.py`, `app/spreadsheet_import/domain/rows.py` and `app/catalog/adapters/import_port.py`.\
      \ Each has a reason, and a test fails if an entry matches nothing.\n  - Pinned\
      \ constants: each changed constant differs from its old Spanish value.\n  -\
      \ It is shown failing on a fixture with a Spanish message, and passing on a\
      \ sheet word.\n- **The marker list.** Create `web/src/architecture/spanishMarkers.json`,\
      \ which holds `{\"characters\": \"áéíóúñü¿¡\", \"words\": [...]}`. Its stoplist\
      \ holds unambiguous Spanish function and verb words: de, del, la, las, los,\
      \ el, que, para, por, con, sin, una, hoy, desde, tu, vos, ya, todavía, acá,\
      \ borrar, guardá, cargá, …\n  - It must not hit the English seeds, \"Packaging\"\
      , or the catalog words.\n  - It is the only file under `web/` this subtask owns.\n\
      - **Upkeep test.** A test through `push(..., \"upkeep\")` with the fake Telegram:\n\
      \  - a pin whose `last_published_text` is the old Spanish render gets exactly\
      \ one edit and no post;\n  - a second push sends nothing;\n  - a pin with `last_failure`\
      \ set is retried;\n  - an English render over the limit takes the existing `TooLong`\
      \ path while the triggering save succeeds.\n\n**Design sample:** `scripts/design/sample_app.py`\
      \ sets the sample business's categories, header and footer through the API to\
      \ what `pantallas/` draws. These are seller data. `muestra.md` stays byte-identical,\
      \ and `tests/design/test_muestra_seed.py` passes unedited.\n\n**Acceptance:**\
      \ pytest, mypy and ruff are green. A new business gets the English defaults\
      \ (API test). The import still matches and adds categories by name. `contract.json`\
      \ and migrations have no diff."
    seams:
    - app/catalog/domain/text.py::catalog_text (catalog reference set)
    - POST /invitations accept → GET /expenses/categories and GET /catalog/settings
      (new business defaults)
    - app/pinned_catalog/application/push.py::push with tests/fakes/telegram
    - tests/architecture/test_app_text.py (AST guard over app/)
    - tests/design/test_muestra_seed.py (unchanged)
    depends_on: []
    status: approved

  text-guards:
    id: text-guards
    title: 'S1b-1: front text guards, ledgers, PENDING markers and the locale ban,
      with ADR-0048'
    brief: "Add the front guards. No wording changes in this subtask.\n\n**1. `web/src/architecture/appText.ts`**\
      \ exports `findVisibleLiterals(path, source)`, built on `ts.createSourceFile`\
      \ from the installed `typescript` package.\n- **Scope:** every non-test `.ts`/`.tsx`\
      \ under `web/src`, except `copy/`, `architecture/`, `test/`, `design/` and `routes.ts`.\n\
      - **It flags** any string literal, no-substitution template, template head,\
      \ middle or tail part, or JSX text that contains a Unicode letter.\n- **Closed\
      \ list of allowed contexts:**\n  - import or export specifiers and dynamic import\
      \ paths;\n  - type positions;\n  - operands of `===`/`!==` and `case` clauses;\n\
      \  - property names and element-access arguments;\n  - the `className` attribute\
      \ and the class helper's arguments;\n  - `data-*` attributes;\n  - the props\
      \ `type`, `role`, `id`, `name`, `htmlFor`, `inputMode`, `enterKeyHint`, `autoComplete`,\
      \ `variant`, `action`, `icon`, `nombre`, `to`, `href`, `textOnly`, `kind`;\n\
      \  - the first argument of `Intl.NumberFormat`.\n\n  A context added later is\
      \ added to this list with its reason, never as a per-file exemption.\n- **Tests.**\
      \ Fixtures as `.txt` files read with `?raw` cover a variable, a ternary, a template,\
      \ a helper return, `document.title` and `window.confirm`, and each must be flagged.\
      \ Comparisons and class names must pass.\n\n**2. Ledgers.**\n- `web/src/architecture/textLeft/<owner>.json`,\
      \ one per owner: `shared` (S1b-2b), `getting-in-settings`, `sales-customers`,\
      \ `varieties-stock-catalog`, `home-analysis-expenses-onboarding`.\n- Assign\
      \ every offending file to exactly one owner. Components, `shell/`, `flash/`,\
      \ `format/`, `api/`, `domain/`, `stock/` and `sales/` go to `shared`.\n- `appText.real.test.ts`\
      \ fails on any offending file not in a ledger, and on any ledger entry that\
      \ no longer offends (shrink-only).\n- A pytest (`tests/test_text_ledgers.py`)\
      \ asserts the ledgers are pairwise disjoint.\n\n**3. Spanish rule over `copy/`**\
      \ (`copySpanish.ts` plus a test).\n- It skips any section exporting `PENDING\
      \ = true`.\n- Otherwise, each value is rendered with `SAMPLES` for functions,\
      \ then NFC-normalised, trimmed and case-folded. It fails when either:\n  - it\
      \ equals any value in `copyBaseline.es.json` (keys ignored), unless that section's\
      \ exported `SAME_IN_BOTH: readonly {value, reason}[]` lists it;\n  - it hits\
      \ `spanishMarkers.json`, read through Vite (the file lives under `web/`).\n\
      - **Fixtures:**\n  - a renamed key keeping \"Guardar\" fails;\n  - a function\
      \ entry rendering a baseline value fails;\n  - an accented new string fails;\n\
      \  - an English string passes.\n\n**4. PENDING.** Add `export const PENDING\
      \ = true` to every section.\n\n**5. Locale ban.** A test fails on `toLocale`\
      \ or `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/`Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` in any non-test file under\
      \ `web/src`.\n\n**6. ADR-0048.** Record the package, the two front guards, the\
      \ backend guard (S2), the ledgers and `PENDING` (both removed in S7), and the\
      \ known risks. Record also that `pantallas/` and `muestra.md` stay Spanish design\
      \ reference: `section` crops run on the design side only.\n\n**Acceptance:**\
      \ vitest is green on the real tree, with every current offender in a ledger.\
      \ Each guard is shown failing on its fixtures."
    seams:
    - web/src/architecture/appText.ts::findVisibleLiterals (fixture tests + real-tree
      test)
    - web/src/architecture/copySpanish.ts (fixture tests + real-tree test)
    - tests/test_text_ledgers.py
    depends_on:
    - copy-move
    - backend-text
    status: approved
    branch: agentic/GH-53.text-guards
    base: d4286d1b
    reviewed_head: 15ce0f6ae1de2fdc90cff0015fa4eb7cbd444c57
  terms-common-format:
    id: terms-common-format
    title: 'S1b-2a: glossary terms, the common section and the words inside formats
      in English'
    brief: "**What to change**\n- **CONTEXT.md.** Rewrite every `_In the app_` line\
      \ in English.\n  - It is the concept's glossary headword (e.g. Variety/Varieties,\
      \ Customer, Sale, Payment, Balance, Credit, Stock withdrawal, Promotion, Own\
      \ use, Stock shortfall, Pending cost, Profit, Cash, Gift, Special price).\n\
      \  - The pricing-rule words are list, bulk, VIP, gift and special.\n  - Where\
      \ there is no headword, propose a word: Home, More, Settings, Business, Users,\
      \ Prices, Integrations, Catalog message, Expense categories, Appearance, Your\
      \ account.\n  - Lines \"no word of its own\" and \"operator terminal only\"\
      \ stay as they are.\n  - Review the `_Avoid_` lists so none forbids a word the\
      \ app now shows.\n  - Change the header sentence (\"the Spanish word\") to \"\
      the English word\".\n- **The `common` section** in English (e.g. Close, Save,\
      \ \"No connection\", \"That's gone.\", \"That's gone. The list is up to date.\"\
      , the retry). Delete its `PENDING`.\n- **`copy/format.ts`** in English: weekday\
      \ and month names, \"Today\" (\"Today 07/10\"), \"since\", \"and N g not yet\
      \ costed\", \"N g not yet costed\". Delete its `PENDING`. `web/src/format/*`\
      \ read their words from there.\n- **Format-lock test** in `web/src/format/`\
      \ pins `$ 11.000`, `−$ 4.500`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, \"Today dd/mm\", \"and N g\
      \ not yet costed\" and \"N g not yet costed\".\n- **Fix every test line and\
      \ e2e line in any file that these words break**, one for one, with nothing loosened.\
      \ List each edited test in the PR.\n\n**Approval:** your reviewer approves the\
      \ CONTEXT.md lines against the headwords. Anything the glossary can't settle\
      \ goes to the PM through this review.\n\n**Acceptance:** vitest, e2e and pytest\
      \ are green. `common` and `format` pass the Spanish rule. `contract.json` has\
      \ no diff."
    seams:
    - web/src/format/*.test.ts (format-lock and date words)
    - web/src/architecture/copySpanish.ts real-tree test over common and format
    - existing screen tests and e2e specs, expectations translated one for one
    depends_on:
    - text-guards
    status: approved
    branch: agentic/GH-53.terms-common-format

  shared-ui:
    id: shared-ui
    title: 'S1b-2b: shared components, the shell, Estados and the document shell in
      English'
    brief: 'Translate everything in the `shared` ledger (`web/src/architecture/textLeft/shared.json`):

      · Varieties · More.


      Then:

      - Move the texts into their sections (`common`, `states`, `shell`) and delete
      the `PENDING` of `states` and `shell`.

      - Empty `textLeft/shared.json`.

      - `web/index.html`: `lang="en"`, an English `<title>` and description. `web/public/manifest.webmanifest`:

      stays `"/"`. Update the installed-shell tests for the new `lang` value only.

      - Fix every test and e2e line these words break, in any file, one for one. List
      each in the PR.

      - Keep the icon-and-text rule: accessible names become the English word, with
      the same closed lists and 44 px targets.


      **Acceptance**

      - The gate is green. The `shared` ledger is empty. `states` and `shell` pass
      the Spanish rule.

      - The bar still fits at 320 px with text at 130 % (`barFit` e2e).

      - The manifest passes installability.'

    brief: 'Owner of `textLeft/getting-in-settings.json`, and of the sections `auth`
      and `settings`.

      - Read every visible text from `copy`.

      - Make a faithful English version: "you" for vos, imperative buttons, glossary
      words from CONTEXT.md.

      - Security texts reveal nothing new: no attempt count and no reason a link is
      dead.

      - Each text keeps its length limit. A text shortened to fit keeps its facts
      and moves the how or why behind More info; list each one in the PR.

      - Import shared words from `common` or another section read-only, never copy
      them.


      **Finish**

      - Delete `PENDING` from your sections and empty your ledger.

      - Translate the tests and e2e lines of these screens one for one (e.g. `passwords.spec.ts`,
      `settings` specs), loosening nothing. List them.

      - Update the quoted copy of `docs/product/accounts.md`, `pricing-settings.md`
      and `pinned-catalog.md` faithfully, plus their "Rioplatense Spanish" lines.

      - Never edit CONTEXT.md, `common` or shared components.


      **Acceptance:** the gate is green, your ledger is empty, and your sections pass
      the Spanish rule and `copyLimits`.'

  close:
    id: close
    title: 'S7: remove the ratchet, turn on the term check, docs, standards, routes
      check and design evidence'
    brief: '**Remove the ratchet**

      - Assert that no section exports `PENDING`, then delete the mechanism and its
      handling in `copySpanish.ts`. The baseline comparison and the marker rule then
      apply to every section.

      - Assert every `textLeft/*.json` is empty, then delete the directory, its loading
      and `tests/test_text_ledgers.py`. The visible-literal rule then has no allowance
      at all.


      **Turn on the term check:** a pytest asserting each `_In the app_` word of CONTEXT.md
      appears verbatim in some value under `web/src/copy/`. It reads the TS sources
      as text.


      **Routes check:** a test that no path segment in `web/src/routes.ts` or `scripts/design/screens.json`
      equals a segment of any `old` path in `app/legacy_front_paths.json`, and that
      every path is ASCII. The redirect table is untouched.


      **Docs**

      - BRIEF.md: "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English".

      - `docs/product/design-system/README.md`: English is the app''s language, and
      `pantallas/` is the reference for structure, not wording.

      - `app-text.md`: drop its precedence note once every capability doc is updated.

      - CODING_STANDARDS §10''s first bullet becomes "Every word on screen is English,
      using the glossary''s `_In the app_` word". The rest stays.

      - Amend ADR-0048 with the removal.


      **Design tooling**

      - The built-side steps of `scripts/design/screens.json` (`click`, `press` and
      `wait` texts that target the built app) become the English the app shows. Design-side
      `section` labels stay Spanish.

      - Seams: `web/src/design/screens.test.ts` and `routes.test.ts` only.


      **Evidence**

      - One whole-registry `comparisons.mjs --strict` run, exit 0, judged by eye on
      phone and computer, both themes.

      - List every clip found at 320 px or 130 % text and fix it CSS-only with existing
      tokens, or file it.

      - Record the fontsource version.


      **PR lists:** every edited test across the task, every shortened text, and every
      `SAME_IN_BOTH` entry with its reason.'
    seams:
    - web/src/architecture/copySpanish.ts and appText.real.test.ts (no allowance)
    - tests/test_app_text_terms.py (CONTEXT.md terms vs copy)
    - tests/test_routes_english.py
    - web/src/design/screens.test.ts
    - web/src/routes.test.ts
    depends_on:
    - screens-getting-in-settings
    - screens-sales-customers
    - screens-varieties-stock-catalog
    - screens-home-analysis-expenses-onboarding
    status: approved
    branch: agentic/GH-53.close
    base: b66b747a
    conflicted_paths: []
    reviewed_head: c819a85637134486b73889f0c87382624d79fb19
    reason: 'Evidence block still partial: about 30 of 156 sheets and 7 of 8 captures
      at 320 px / 130 % were viewed, and the two clips that were not fixed are noted
      only in the Developer''s report. The CSS fix and the e2e fix are verified (the
      spec passes alone).'
    return_category: spec-scope

    brief: 'S7 does the close-out: it removes the ratchet (the PENDING markers and

invariants:
- web/src/api/contract.json has no diff in any subtask.
- No file under app/migrations/ changes, and no stored row is rewritten; only the
  defaults for a new business change.
- The formats `$ 11.000`, `−$ 4.500`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm` and the catalog's `$11.000`
  and `2,5 g` stay byte-identical.
- No test assertion is dropped, loosened or turned into a pattern; an expectation
  is translated one for one, and the PR lists every edited test.
- Behaviour tests assert the English text a person sees, by role and accessible name,
  never by reading a copy key.
- No `toLocale*`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat` or `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` appears anywhere
  in non-test web/src.
- A text-ledger file (web/src/architecture/textLeft/*.json) only shrinks.
- A screen-group subtask edits only its own copy sections, its own ledger, its own
  screens, their tests and e2e lines, and its own capability docs; it never edits
  CONTEXT.md, the common section, a shared component or another group's files.
- A word shared across sections is imported read-only from the section that owns it,
  never copied.
- Every label that names a glossary concept uses that concept's `_In the app_` word
  in CONTEXT.md.
- No section value outside its SAME_IN_BOTH list equals a value in copyBaseline.es.json,
  whatever its key; SAME_IN_BOTH entries carry a reason.
- 'No path is exempt from the backend text guard except the closed list: app/migrations/**,
  spreadsheet_import adapters/layout.py, cells.py, domain/rows.py, catalog adapters/import_port.py.'
- The import accepts exactly the sheet titles and values it reads today.
- muestra.md and pantallas/ are not edited.
integration_brief: 'S7 does the close-out: it removes the ratchet (the PENDING markers
  and textLeft/), turns on the CONTEXT.md term check, rewrites CODING_STANDARDS §10''s
  first bullet and the design README, updates the built-side steps of screens.json,
  and attaches the whole-registry design capture. The screen-group branches must be
  merged into the task branch before S7 starts.'

design_items:
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Collect payment · Start (Cobro.dc.html)
  text: The flow says the job starts 'from the sale', but VentaDetalle.dc.html has
    no link to Cobro. In the prototype Cobro is reached from Inicio's owes-you list
    and from ClienteFicha. The start point should be corrected.
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Check and adjust stock · Start
  text: The prototype has no 'stock screen'. Stock is reached from Inicio's low-stock
    rows or Variedades, through VariedadDetalle to EntradaStock or TodasLasEntradas,
    and withdrawals go through SacarDelStock (which reuses EntradaStock's design).
    The flow names a screen that does not exist.
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Catalog, import and Telegram · Import
  text: The flow starts from 'the import screen'. import.md says the import is an
    operator script, not a route or a screen, and that the report exists only on the
    operator's terminal. The job holds (fix the file, run it again), but the flow
    names a screen that does not exist and should describe the operator's terminal
    instead. Sin prototipo.
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Coverage of jobs
  text: 'The flow leaves out several jobs whose texts also change: onboarding (NegocioNuevo,
    Invitacion/Reset, Onboarding*), expenses (Gastos, GastoNuevo), Análisis, varieties
    and prices (Variedades, VariedadNueva, VerTodas, PrecioNuevo, archived varieties).
    Since nothing changes but wording, none of these is blocked, but the flow claims
    to cover every place a person meets text.'
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Settings · existing business with Spanish seeds
  text: 'The prototype confirms the edit path: ConfiguracionCategorias offers Renombrá
    per category, and ConfiguracionMensaje (reachable from Catalogo) lets the seller
    edit the header and footer. Mixed language is friction for existing businesses
    until they edit, with nothing prompting them, as the PRD decided.'
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Design comparisons · every screen
  text: The prototype stays in Spanish. The screens.json steps click and fill by exact
    Spanish text ('Entrá', 'Contraseña', 'Semana', 'Elegir cliente', 'Guardá la venta'),
    and their section labels point at Spanish blocks of the design. After this change,
    steps must use the English app's words while sections keep the design's Spanish
    labels. From here on, comparisons check structure only, never wording.
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Record a sale · English length
  text: English copy is often longer than the Spanish. The 80-character help-line
    limit and the 40-character empty-state title limit apply, with overflow moved
    behind More info. That changes where a fact is read, not a step. Worth checking
    by eye on the narrow phone screens (NuevaVenta, Estados).
- role_slug: ux_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Telegram · pinned catalog
  text: 'Sin prototipo. Judged on the text alone: the edit in place at the next upkeep
    adds no step and no new message, so the job holds. A catalog may mix English fixed
    words with a stored Spanish header and footer.'
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Reset (password reset)
  text: 'The screens design says Reset ''reuses the Invitación screen'', but the built
    /reset/{token} is a smaller screen: a ''choose your new password'' title, one
    password field, Show, and ''Save and sign in''. Nothing on it says who invited
    you, and the expiry line is missing. The registry pairs it with Invitacion.dc.html
    as a reference only. The English must follow the built Reset screen''s own elements,
    not Invitación''s copy.'
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Inicio · setup progress card and pending-cost marks
  text: 'The built Inicio has the ''continue with setup · N of 6 steps · Missing:
    …'' card and the ''incluye costo pendiente'' marks under Profit and Own use. The
    Inicio entry names neither. Both are app text and must come in English from the
    keyed module, with the card''s notice line at 80 characters or less.'
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Inicio · period tabs, stock badges, bottom bar
  text: 'The design leaves unspecified which words English makes longer: the tabs
    (Today / Week / Month), the badges (ok, low, no entries, sold out) and the five
    bottom-bar labels (Home, Sales, Customers, Varieties, More). They must stay on
    one line inside their current pill and tab sizes. Check this by eye on the phone
    width.'
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Mas, VerTodas, VariedadNueva, InicioPrimeraVez
  text: These screens are in the prototype but not listed one by one. The general
    rule ('each product screen applied as is, words only') covers them, so nothing
    is missing from the design. The wording of their states is simply not written
    down.
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Catálogo · empty state
  text: The screens design gives the Catalogo screen an empty state reading 'Nothing
    to offer for now.'. In the PRD that line is the catalog text the bot posts when
    nothing qualifies, not necessarily the screen's empty-state title. If the screen's
    empty state has its own copy, it needs its own faithful English, with the title
    at 40 characters or less.
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Variedades / stock screens
  text: The design says stock is reached through the variety screens, 'not from the
    Variedades list directly'. Inicio's 'what you have left' rows also lead there.
    This is a navigation note, not part of the screens design, and it changes nothing
    to build.
- role_slug: ui_lead
  kind: observation
  step: Non-screen surfaces (manifest, page title, Telegram, import report)
  text: No drawing covers these, and none is needed. They are words only, and the
    PRD's metrics and the gate guard check them. Recorded here so nobody expects a
    design comparison for them.
- role_slug: pm_consolidator
  kind: observation
  step: Collect payment (flow)
  text: The flow says payment starts "from the sale", but no sale detail links to
    Registrar cobro. It is reached from Inicio's and Clientes' debtor rows and from
    the customer's card. The job still completes within its budget.
- role_slug: pm_consolidator
  kind: observation
  step: Check and adjust stock (flow)
  text: The flow names a "stock screen" that does not exist. Stock is reached from
    Inicio's low-stock rows or from Variedades → a variety's page → the stock entry
    form, All entries or Take out of stock.
- role_slug: pm_consolidator
  kind: observation
  step: Catalog, import and Telegram (flow)
  text: The flow starts the import from "the import screen", but the import is an
    operator script with no screen. Its report exists only on the terminal. The recovery
    (fix the file, run it again) holds.
- role_slug: pm_consolidator
  kind: observation
  step: Flow coverage
  text: The flow does not list Onboarding, Expenses, Análisis, Varieties and prices,
    or Archived varieties. Their words change too, and nothing in them is blocked.

# The whole app in English: routes and every text a person reads

## Original request

Blocked by: #45, #46, #47, #48, #49

Waits for the screens to be rebuilt as designed (#45, #46, #47, #48, #49) and for Análisis (#8): they rewrite most of the texts this issue changes, and they compare against designs written in Spanish. The `agentic` label goes on once they are merged.

########## Current behaviour

Zeta, 2026-10-04: the app's routes and texts are in Spanish (`/ventas`, `/clientes`, "Nueva venta", "Te debe", "Guardá"...). The design (`docs/product/design-system/`) is written in Rioplatense Spanish too.

########## Expected

The app is in English by default, all of it:

- **Routes**: already renamed to English by the navigation redesign (part 2, after #58); nothing to do here beyond checking none is left in Spanish.
- **Texts**: every text a person reads in the front (titles, labels, buttons, empty and error states, confirmations), the messages the API returns for a person to read, and what the Telegram bot posts.
- **One place for the texts**: the front's texts live in one module, keyed, not scattered in the screens. No translation library and no language switch yet: Zeta will adjust the wording and decide on translations later, and this makes that a change in one place.
- **Formats stay**: money stays in pesos and dates stay day-month-year; only the words change.
- **Design docs**: `docs/product/design-system/README.md` says the app's language is English and the screens in `pantallas/` are the reference for structure, not for the words. `scripts/design/screens.json` follows the new routes.
- Tests and e2e follow the new texts and routes.

########## Out of scope

Translations, a language switch, and rewording beyond a faithful English version of each text.

## PjM

The app's visible texts (front, API messages, Telegram bot) change from Spanish to English, so users see different words, and the design docs change with them. The single-module text store is a side effect of that.

## Interview: Product Analyst→PM

### Round 1: questions

### Round 1: the job to be done

Context: GH-53 moves the whole app (screens, API messages shown to people, Telegram posts) from Rioplatense Spanish to English. BRIEF.md says today: "Interface in Rioplatense Spanish; code and docs in English". This round only settles the job. Later rounds are about behaviour that serves it.

❓ **Q1** - **Who reaches for this, and in which situation?**: Who needs the app in English, and when do they notice? Examples: a seller or helper who doesn't read Spanish, the team demoing or testing the product, or the design docs and tests that today mix both languages.
➡️ Recommendation: Job story: "When I open any screen, read an API message or receive a Telegram post, I want all of it in plain English, so I can understand and use the app without translating, and the team works from one language across product, design and tests."

❓ **Q2** - **What progress do they want?**: Is the goal only that the words change (a faithful English version of what exists), or also a change of tone or wording? The issue puts rewording beyond faithful English out of scope.
➡️ Recommendation: Faithful English only. The same meaning, the same screens, the same flows. No new copy, no new features, no translations, no language switch.

❓ **Q3** - **How will you judge it went well?**: What proves it is done?
➡️ Recommendation: Three criteria:
1. No Spanish text is visible anywhere a person reads: screens, refusal messages, catalog text and Telegram posts.
2. Behaviour and formats are unchanged: pesos like `$ 11.000` and day-month-year dates behave as today, and no route is in Spanish.
3. Design docs, `screens.json`, tests and e2e agree with the English app, and the README says English is the app language.

❓ **Q4** - **What do people do today without it?**: Today users read Spanish copy, and the team keeps design docs and tests that assert exact Spanish wording. Is that the pain you want removed?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes. Name the pain as "the app only speaks Spanish, and the design docs and tests are tied to Spanish wording." This is the baseline the criteria in Q3 are measured against.

### Round 1: answers

### Round 1: the job to be done

**Q1**: Accepted, with the protagonist named. The decision comes from Zeta, who owns the product. The job serves two people:

- **The person reading the app.** The seller on the phone, their partner, and the customers who read the catalog in Telegram all read English by default.
- **Zeta, as the person who owns the wording.** Zeta wants every text a person reads in one place, so that adjusting a word or adding a translation later is a change in one place, not a search through screens.

The job story:

**When** I open any screen, read a message the app or the API shows me, or read the catalog the bot posts, **I want** all of it in plain English, with every text kept in one place, **so I can** use the app without translating anything, and the product's owner can change any wording, or later add a language, in one place without touching the screens.

This refines every capability's "run my business from my phone" job. It is not a new capability. It adds no behaviour, screen, route or step.

BRIEF.md's constraint "Interface in Rioplatense Spanish" is replaced by "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English". The request is Zeta's explicit decision, and no ADR stands in the way: no ADR fixes the interface language. Every capability doc's "Rioplatense Spanish with vos" line changes the same way.

Assumption: existing sellers, who read Spanish, accept an English app until Zeta decides on translations. The request accepts this when it defers translations and a language switch.

**Q2**: Accepted. The goal is a faithful English version of what exists: the same meaning, screens, flows, actions, confirmations and facts named in each message.

- No new copy, no rewording beyond faithful English, no translation library and no language switch.
- Where a Spanish text names a fact (a date, an amount, grams, a reason), the English text names the same fact.
- The security texts keep their guarantees (the brake message, the Entrar hint, the dead-link texts): they reveal nothing more than today.
- The icon-and-text rule's length limits still hold on the English copy (80 characters for a help line, 40 for an empty-state title), counted the same way.

**Q3**: Accepted, with two additions and the boundary of what counts as "text a person reads". The success criteria:

1. **No Spanish where a person reads.** No Spanish word the app itself writes shows anywhere a person reads: every screen, toast, confirmation, empty, loading and error state, refusal message, the accessible names of controls, the catalog text, the default catalog header and footer for a new business, and what the Telegram bot posts. Not counted as the app's text, and never translated:
   - what a seller typed: variety, customer and category names, notes, aromas, and the catalog header and footer already stored for an existing business;
   - the spreadsheet's own words that the import reads, such as its column titles.
2. **One place for the texts.** Every text the front shows lives in one keyed module, and no screen writes its own literal. Changing a word means editing one entry, and that edit changes every screen that shows it.
3. **Nothing else changes.** Behaviour, flows, tap budgets, isolation and refusal codes stay as they are. Formats stay too: money in pesos as `$ 11.000` and `−$ 4.500`, the catalog's own `$11.000`, grams as `3,5 g`, dates as `dd/mm`. No route is in Spanish, and the old Spanish paths still redirect.
4. **One language across the product docs.** The design-system README says English is the app's language and that `pantallas/` is the reference for structure, not for words. `screens.json`, the capability docs' copy, CONTEXT.md's "In the app" words, the tests and the e2e all agree with the English app. No test is loosened to get there.

**Q4**: Accepted, with the pain named. Today:

- the app speaks only Spanish;
- its texts are scattered through the screens;
- the design files, the docs and the tests are tied to exact Spanish wording.

So any change of wording, or a future translation, means hunting through screens and tests. That is the baseline the criteria in Q3 are measured against.

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2: behaviour that serves the job

The job is settled. These questions cover the edges of "all in English, nothing else changes".

❓ **Q1** - **Seeded names that are already stored**: Some names are written into each business when it is created, such as the expense categories ("Insumos de cultivo") and the default catalog header and footer. You said what a seller typed, and the header and footer already stored, are not translated. Are seeded names the same case? Existing businesses would keep their Spanish category names, and new businesses would get English ones.
➡️ Recommendation: Yes. Treat a stored seeded name as seller data and leave it alone. Only the seed for new businesses changes to English. There is no migration that rewrites existing rows. An existing seller may see a mix of languages until the translations decision, and the PRD should say so.

❓ **Q2** - **API messages nobody reads on screen**: The front maps refusal codes to texts (ADR-0008) and never reads `detail`. The `detail` text is still part of the API's output. Should it become English with everything else?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes. Every message the API writes is English, including `detail`, so no Spanish is left in the product's output. Refusal codes and the contract stay as they are.

❓ **Q3** - **Tone of the English**: Today's copy uses vos, in a short and warm Rioplatense register. What register should the English have?
➡️ Recommendation: Plain, short English. It is direct ("you") only where the Spanish was, and uses imperative verbs on buttons. It is neutral and friendly, with no slang and no added warmth or humour. The Spanish copy sets the length and meaning, within the limits you gave.

❓ **Q4** - **Domain words**: CONTEXT.md gives each concept an English term and a Spanish "In the app" word. Which English word does the app show for each concept?
➡️ Recommendation: Use the glossary's English term as the app word, so the front, docs and code use the same word. Where the glossary has no clear English term, I propose one in the PRD and update CONTEXT.md. The "In the app" column is then removed or replaced by the English word.

❓ **Q5** - **Words inside formats**: You kept the formats (`$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`). Some displays also spell words out, such as weekday or month names, and the ordering of names. Do those follow the language?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes for words: weekday and month names, "today" and "yesterday", and unit words appear in English. No for numbers and symbols: separators, the `$` placement, the decimal comma and the `dd/mm` order stay exactly as they are. Sorting of names stays as it is today.

### Round 2: answers

### Round 2: behaviour that serves the job

**Q1**: Accepted. A seeded name that is already stored is the seller's data from the moment it is written: the seller can rename or remove it like anything they typed. Only the seed for a new business changes:
- the four default expense categories, in faithful English;
- the default catalog header and footer, in faithful English, keeping their emoji and `**` markers.

No migration rewrites existing rows. Category matching keeps its rules: the spreadsheet import matches categories by name and adds any that are missing (import.md). The PRD states that an existing business may show its stored Spanish names and its Spanish header and footer next to the English interface, and that this is expected until the translations decision. Nothing in the app prompts the seller to change them.

Assumption: the English defaults are a plain rendering of the Spanish ones, such as "Growing supplies", "Packaging", "Shipping and travel", "Other" and "🌿 **CATALOG** 🌿". The exact words are the PRD's to write under Q3's register.

**Q2**: Accepted. Every message the API writes is in English, `detail` included. ADR-0008 already makes `detail` an English string from the domain, so this mostly confirms the current state and catches any leftover Spanish. These stay unchanged:
- every refusal code and its kind;
- `contract.json`;
- ADR-0005's rule that a refusal never repeats the input.

The front still never reads `detail`. Whatever an operator command prints for the operator is in English too.

**Q3**: Accepted. The English is plain and short:
- it addresses the reader as "you" where the Spanish used vos;
- buttons are imperative verbs ("Save sale", "Collect");
- it is neutral and friendly, with no slang, and no warmth or humour beyond what the Spanish had.

Each English text keeps the facts its Spanish one names. It fits the icon-and-text rule's length limits, counted the same way on the English copy. A text the limit forces to shorten keeps its facts and moves only the how or why behind More info, as the rule allows. The security texts reveal nothing more than today.

**Q4**: Accepted, with one distinction:
- **A label that names a concept** (a tab, a heading, a badge, a field) uses the glossary's English term, so the front, the docs and the code share one word. Examples: Varieties, Customers, Sales, Expenses, Balance, Credit, Payment, Gift, Special price, Promotion, Own use, Stock shortfall, Pending cost, Profit, Cash.
- **A sentence** is translated faithfully and uses those terms inside it ("owes you since dd/mm", "Nobody owes you").

Where a glossary term is a poor label on screen, or an "In the app" word has no clear glossary twin, the PRD proposes the English word, and CONTEXT.md is updated in the same change. A glossary term marked "no word of its own" stays without one.

CONTEXT.md keeps its "_In the app_" line, which now holds the English word the seller sees. That way the next language change edits one known line per term. The "_Avoid_" lists are reviewed so they don't forbid a word the app now shows.

**Q5**: Accepted:
- **Words follow the language**: weekday and month names, "Today" (as in "Today 07/10"), "since", and any word inside a figure ("y N g sin costo" becomes "and N g not yet costed", or the PRD's equivalent).
- **Numbers, symbols and their order stay as they are**: the thousands dot, the decimal comma, the `$ ` prefix and its space, the minus sign, `g`, `dd/mm`, and the catalog's own `$11.000` and `2,5 g`.
- **Sorting and search stay as they are**: by name, ignoring case and accents. Monday stays the first day of the week.

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3: the last edges

You settled the language, tone, terms and formats. These four questions cover the remaining places where Spanish could hide or where "unchanged" needs a decision.

❓ **Q1** - **Text around the screens**: Some texts a person reads sit outside the screens: the page title, the installed-app name and description, the page's declared language, the link previews, and the file names of anything the app lets you download or share. Do they switch to English too?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes. All of them are English, and the page's declared language becomes English, so phone readers and spell-checkers treat the app as English. The installed-app name stays the product's name ("Apostrophe"), which has no Spanish form.

❓ **Q2** - **Spreadsheet import**: The import reads the seller's spreadsheet by its Spanish column titles. You said those words are not the app's text. Should the import also accept English titles, so an English-speaking seller can bring a sheet?
➡️ Recommendation: No. The import is unchanged and accepts the same titles as today. English titles would be a new behaviour, which is outside this faithful-English change. The texts the import shows around the sheet (instructions, the result report, refusals) are English.

❓ **Q3** - **Posts already sent**: Telegram posts already published stay as they were sent. From now on, every post the bot writes is English, including the "nothing to offer yet" message and the reasons Probarlo shows. Is that the whole behaviour for the customer-facing side, with no per-business choice?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes. No past post is edited or re-sent, and there is no per-business language choice. A business with a stored Spanish header and footer shows them as stored (Round 2, Q1), so its catalog may mix languages until the translations decision. The PRD records this.

❓ **Q4** - **Proof that no Spanish is left**: You said no test may be loosened to get there. Should "done" include a check that keeps Spanish from coming back, so a later change can't add a screen with its own literal text or a Spanish message?
➡️ Recommendation: Yes, as a lightweight check. It fails when a screen writes a text of its own instead of using the keyed module, and when an app-written message contains Spanish. It ignores seller data and the import's column titles. It does not add a translation library or a language switch.

### Round 3: answers

### Round 3: the last edges

**Q1**: Accepted. The page title, the installed app's name and description, the page's declared language and the manifest's `lang` all become English (`en`). The installed app's name stays "Apostrophe".

One rule from accounts.md is kept exactly as it is: the manifest's `id` stays `"/"`, so an installed app is never reinstalled because of this change. Only the manifest's `lang` and its words change, and the manifest still has to pass the browser's installability check.

The app offers no download and shares no file today. The catalog is shared as text, and the business export is the operator's command. There are no file names to translate, and none are added.

**Q2**: Accepted. The import accepts exactly the column titles, cell values and fixed cells it reads today: "Compra", "Pendiente", "Amigo", "Sí"/"No" and the rest. Accepting English titles would be new behaviour, which is out of scope.

The import has no screen and nothing a seller reads. Its report and refusals live on the operator's terminal and are written in English. When the report lists an unfinished row, it quotes the sheet's own text as it appears in the sheet (e.g. `Falta pago`), because that is the sheet's data.

**Q3**: Accepted, with one correction about the pinned message. No past post is re-sent, and there is no per-business language choice.

The pinned catalog is edited in place by upkeep, so it does not stay frozen:
- once the change ships, the catalog text is built in English: the price and bulk lines, and "Nothing to offer for now." when nothing qualifies;
- the next upkeep, at the latest the next Argentina day change, finds the text differs from the last one published and edits the pinned message into English, as its rules already say;
- nothing new is built for this: no forced resend and no new post.

A business with a stored Spanish header and footer keeps them, so its catalog may mix languages until the translations decision. The PRD records this.

Probarlo's and Buscar mi chat's reasons, and the pin's statuses, are app text shown on screen. They are English under the job's first criterion.

**Q4**: Accepted, as a guard in the gate that keeps the job's first two criteria true. What it must guarantee:
- a screen that writes its own visible text, instead of reading it from the keyed module, fails;
- Spanish found in the keyed module, in the API's messages or in the catalog and bot texts fails.

It must never flag:
- seller data;
- the import's sheet words;
- the formats kept on purpose (`3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, `$ 11.000`);
- the product name.

It must also be fast, give the same verdict on any date, and pass over the real tree with no false alarm. A real offender found while building it is fixed, never exempted. The existing copy guards, the length limits and their registration, still run on the English copy with no assertion loosened.

How it detects Spanish, and where it runs, is the Tech Lead's decision.

### Closing

### Closing summary

**Job (settled):** When a person opens any screen, reads a message from the app or the API, or reads the catalog the bot posts, they want all of it in plain English, with every text kept in one place. They can then use the app without translating, and the owner can change any wording, or later add a language, in one place.

**Decided:**
- **Scope:** Faithful English only, with the same meaning, screens, flows, facts and security guarantees. There is no new copy, no translation library and no language switch. Refusal codes, `contract.json` and the formats stay (`$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, the catalog's `$11.000`). Spanish paths still redirect.
- **Seeded and stored names:** Only the seed for new businesses changes: the four expense categories and the default catalog header and footer. Stored rows are the seller's data and nothing rewrites them. An existing business may mix languages until the translations decision, and the PRD records this.
- **API messages:** Every message the API writes is English, `detail` included. Operator output is English too.
- **Register:** Plain, short, neutral and friendly English. "You" is used where the Spanish used vos, and buttons use imperative verbs. The icon-and-text length limits hold on the English copy, and the security texts reveal nothing more than today.
- **Terms:** A label that names a concept uses the glossary's English term. CONTEXT.md's "_In the app_" line holds the English word the seller sees. Where the glossary has no clear English term, the PRD proposes one, and the "_Avoid_" lists are reviewed.
- **Words in formats:** Weekday and month names, "Today" and "since" are English. Numbers, symbols, their order, sorting and search stay as they are. Monday stays the first day of the week.
- **Around the screens:** The page title, the installed app's description, the page's declared language and the manifest's `lang` become English. The installed app's name stays "Apostrophe", and the manifest `id` stays `"/"`. No downloads or shared files exist today, so there are no file names to translate.
- **Import:** It accepts the same Spanish sheet words as today and gains no English titles. The report quotes the sheet's own text as it appears.
- **Telegram:** The catalog text is built in English. The pinned message is edited into English at the next upkeep, and the next Argentina day change at the latest, with no forced resend. Posts already sent are untouched. There is no per-business language choice. A stored Spanish header and footer stay as stored.
- **Guard:** The gate gets a guard that fails when a screen writes its own visible text, or when Spanish appears in the keyed module, the API messages or the catalog and bot texts. It never flags seller data, the import's sheet words, the kept formats or the product name. A real offender is fixed, never exempted. Existing guards stay, with no assertion loosened. How it detects Spanish, and where it runs, is the Tech Lead's decision.
- **Docs:** The BRIEF constraint becomes "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English". Each capability doc's "Rioplatense Spanish with vos" line changes the same way. The design-system README says English is the app language and `pantallas/` are structure references, not wording. `screens.json`, the capability docs' copy, CONTEXT.md, the tests and the e2e follow the English app.

**Success criteria:**
1. No Spanish the app itself writes shows anywhere a person reads. Seller data and the import's sheet words are excluded.
2. Every front text lives in one keyed module, and no screen writes its own literal.
3. Behaviour, flows, refusal codes and formats are unchanged.
4. The design docs, the glossary, `screens.json`, the tests and the e2e agree with the English app. No test is loosened.

**With the human:** Nothing is pending. No question went unanswered that would need escalation.

## Product Analyst

### Job to be done

**Job story.** When I open any screen, read a message the app or the API shows me, or read the catalog the bot posts, I want all of it in plain English, with every text kept in one place, so I can use the app without translating anything, and the product's owner can change any wording, or later add a language, in one place without touching the screens.

- **Protagonists.** The person reading the app: the seller on the phone, their partner, and the customers who read the catalog in Telegram. Also Zeta, who owns the wording.
- **What it refines.** Every capability's "run my business from my phone" job. It adds no capability, screen, route or step.
- **Pain today.** The app speaks only Spanish. Its texts are scattered through the screens. The design files, docs and tests are tied to exact Spanish wording. Any rewording or future translation means hunting through screens and tests.
- **Constraint that changes.** BRIEF.md's "Interface in Rioplatense Spanish; code and docs in English" becomes "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English". Each capability doc's "Rioplatense Spanish with vos" line changes the same way. No ADR fixes the interface language.
- **Assumption.** Existing sellers, who read Spanish, accept an English app until Zeta decides on translations.

**Success criteria (the user's own).**
1. No Spanish the app itself writes shows anywhere a person reads. That covers:
   - every screen, toast, confirmation, and empty, loading and error state;
   - refusal messages and the accessible names of controls;
   - the catalog text, the default header and footer of a new business, and what the Telegram bot posts.

   Seller data and the import's sheet words are excluded.
2. Every front text lives in one keyed module, and no screen writes its own literal. Changing a word means editing one entry.
3. Behaviour, flows, tap budgets, isolation, refusal codes and formats are unchanged. No route is in Spanish, and the old Spanish paths still redirect.
4. The design docs, CONTEXT.md, `screens.json`, the tests and the e2e agree with the English app. No test is loosened to get there.

**Asked for, and left out.**
- Translations, a language switch and a translation library.
- Rewording beyond a faithful English version.
- English column titles for the spreadsheet import.
- A per-business language choice.
- A forced resend of the pinned message, or editing posts already sent.
- A migration that rewrites stored seeded names.
- Downloads or file names (the app has none).

### Competitors

I have no real data on how other products handle this, so I am not inventing any. Reading the job, three criteria come from the product itself:
- The English must be a faithful rendering of what exists, with no new copy.
- Texts must live in one place, so wording or a language can change later without touching screens.
- The seller-facing language must be consistent across screen, API message and Telegram post.

### Behaviour definition

**1. Scope of the change**
- Every word the app itself writes is English. This covers the front (titles, labels, buttons, tabs, headings, badges, empty, loading and error states, confirmations, toasts, accessible names), every message the API writes (`detail` included), the catalog text, the Telegram bot's posts and the operator commands' output.
- It is a faithful English version: same meaning, screens, flows, actions and confirmations. Where a Spanish text names a fact (date, amount, grams, reason), the English text names the same fact.
- Refusal codes, their kinds and `contract.json` are unchanged. A refusal never repeats the input (ADR-0005). The front still never reads `detail` (ADR-0008).
- Routes are already English after the navigation redesign. This change only verifies that none is left in Spanish. Old Spanish paths keep redirecting permanently.

**2. Register**
- Plain, short, neutral, friendly English. No slang, and no warmth or humour beyond what the Spanish had.
- It addresses the reader as "you" where the Spanish used vos.
- Buttons are imperative verbs (e.g. "Save sale", "Collect").
- The security texts (brake message, Entrar hint, dead-link texts) keep their guarantees and reveal nothing more than today: no attempt count, no reason a link is dead, no id, name or date in "That's gone".
- The icon-and-text rule's length limits hold on the English copy, counted the same way (80 characters for a help line, 40 for an empty-state title). A text the limit forces to shorten keeps its facts, and only the how or why moves behind More info.

**3. Terms**
- A label that names a concept (tab, heading, badge, field) uses the glossary's English term. Examples: Varieties, Customers, Sales, Expenses, Balance, Credit, Payment, Gift, Special price, Promotion, Own use, Stock shortfall, Pending cost, Profit, Cash.
- A sentence is translated faithfully and uses those terms inside it (e.g. "owes you since dd/mm", "Nobody owes you").
- CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` line for each term holds the English word the seller sees. A term marked "no word of its own" stays without one.
- Where the glossary has no clear English term, or an "In the app" word has no glossary twin, the proposed word goes in CONTEXT.md in the same change. The `_Avoid_` lists are reviewed so they do not forbid a word the app now shows.

**4. Words inside formats**
- Words follow the language: weekday and month names, "Today" (as in "Today 07/10"), "since", and any word inside a figure. For example, "y N g sin costo" becomes the English equivalent "and N g not yet costed".
- Numbers and symbols stay exactly as they are: the thousands dot, the decimal comma, `$ 11.000`, `−$ 4.500`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, and the catalog's own `$11.000` and `2,5 g`.
- Sorting and search stay as they are (by name, ignoring case and accents). Monday stays the first day of the week.

**5. Seeded and stored names**
- Only the seed for a new business changes:
  - the four default expense categories, in faithful English (proposed: "Growing supplies", "Packaging", "Shipping and travel", "Other");
  - the default catalog header and footer, in faithful English, keeping their emoji and `**` markers (proposed header: "🌿 **CATALOG** 🌿").
- Rows already stored are the seller's data and nothing rewrites them: no migration. This holds for existing category names and for an existing business's header and footer.
- Assumption (decided): an existing business may show its Spanish category names, header and footer next to the English interface, until the translations decision. Nothing in the app prompts the seller to change them.
- The spreadsheet import still matches categories by name and adds any that are missing. Its rules do not change.

**6. Seller data and sheet words are not translated**
- Never translated: what a seller typed (variety, customer and category names, notes, aromas, a stored header and footer).
- Never translated: the words the import reads from the spreadsheet ("Compra", "Pendiente", "Amigo", "Sí"/"No", column titles and the rest).

**7. Spreadsheet import**
- It accepts exactly the titles, values and fixed cells it reads today. It gains no English titles.
- Its report and refusals are on the operator's terminal and are in English.
- When the report lists an unfinished row, it quotes the sheet's own text as it appears (e.g. `Falta pago`), since that is the sheet's data.

**8. Around the screens**
- The page title, the installed app's description, the page's declared language and the manifest's `lang` become English (`en`).
- The installed app's name stays "Apostrophe". The manifest `id` stays `"/"` (accounts.md), so no installed app is reinstalled, and the manifest still passes the browser's installability check.
- The app offers no downloads and shares no file today. There are no file names to translate and none are added.

**9. Telegram**
- Catalog text built after this ships is English: price and bulk lines, and "Nothing to offer for now." when nothing qualifies. Probarlo's and Buscar mi chat's reasons and the pin's statuses are English on screen.
- Posts already sent are not re-sent or touched, and there is no per-business language choice.
- The pinned message is edited in place by the existing upkeep. At the next upkeep, or at the latest the next Argentina day change, the text differs from the last one published and the message is edited into English. Nothing new is built for this: no forced resend and no new post.
- A business with a stored Spanish header and footer keeps them, so its catalog may mix languages until the translations decision.

**10. One place for the texts**
- Every text the front shows lives in one keyed module. No screen writes its own visible literal. There is no translation library and no language switch.
- Changing one entry changes every screen that shows it.
- Existing copy registration and limit checks (`LIMITED_COPY`, `LONG_REFUSALS`, `SAMPLES`) keep running on the English copy with no assertion loosened.

**11. Guard in the gate**
- A guard keeps criteria 1 and 2 true. It fails when:
  - a screen writes its own visible text instead of reading the keyed module;
  - Spanish appears in the keyed module, in the API's messages, or in the catalog and bot texts.
- It never flags seller data, the import's sheet words, the kept formats (`3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, `$ 11.000`) or the product name "Apostrophe".
- It is fast, gives the same verdict on any date, and passes over the real tree with no false alarm.
- A real offender found while building it is fixed, never exempted.
- How it detects Spanish, and where it runs, is the Tech Lead's decision.

**12. Docs, design and tests**
- The design-system README says English is the app's language and that `pantallas/` are references for structure, not wording.
- `scripts/design/screens.json` follows the routes. Copy-keyed design guards follow the English labels.
- The capability docs' quoted copy, CONTEXT.md, the tests and the e2e follow the English app. No test is loosened.

**Empty states and errors.** None are new. Every existing empty, loading and error state keeps its meaning and conditions and is shown in English.

### Metrics and dashboard

There is no telemetry. Everything below is read from tests and from the gate, derived from the success criteria.

- **No Spanish shown (criterion 1).** The guard passes over the real tree:
  - 0 Spanish strings in the keyed module, the API messages and the catalog and bot texts;
  - the guard is shown failing on a deliberately added Spanish message, and passing on seller data, the import's sheet words and the kept formats.
- **One place (criterion 2).** The guard shows 0 screens with a visible literal of their own. A test over a changed entry shows every screen that uses it changes.
- **Nothing else changes (criterion 3).**
  - Every refusal code in `contract.json` is unchanged.
  - Money, grams and date formats are unchanged.
  - Every old Spanish path still answers its permanent redirect.
  - A scan finds no Spanish route in `screens.json` or the front's registry.
  - The manifest test: `lang` is `en`, `id` is `"/"`, and installability still passes.
  - The page title and the document's declared language are English.
- **Telegram.** A text test shows the catalog, including the empty message, built in English. An upkeep test shows a published Spanish text is edited once in place into English with no new post.
- **Seeds.** A test shows a new business gets the English categories and header and footer, and that existing rows are untouched after the change. The import still matches and adds categories by name.
- **Length limits.** The existing copy-limit tests pass on the English copy, with every shortened text and where its long form went listed in the PR.
- **Docs agree (criterion 4).** README, CONTEXT.md `_In the app_` lines, `screens.json`, capability docs, tests and e2e agree; no test is loosened, listed in the PR.
- **Pain removed (baseline).** The count of texts defined outside the keyed module goes from many to 0.

### Flags

- `touches_ui`: yes. The words of every screen change, and the page title and manifest text a person sees on install change too.
- `touches_copy`: yes. The change rewrites all user-facing text.

### Verdict

`approved`. The PRD matches every decision from the three interview rounds and serves the settled job: the whole app in English, with its texts in one place. The remaining gaps were small, and I closed them myself below.

### Adjustments

1. **The default footer had no wording.** The PRD proposed English for the four default categories and the default header, but not the footer. I added it: "📩 Questions and orders by private message." It keeps its emoji, like the header.
2. **I confirmed the proposed wording** under the decided register:
   - categories: "Growing supplies", "Packaging", "Shipping and travel", "Other";
   - header: "🌿 **CATALOG** 🌿";
   - empty catalog: "Nothing to offer for now.";
   - pending mark: "and N g not yet costed".
3. **The catalog's fixed words are app text.** Words such as "índica / sativa", "Aroma/sabor", "Precio x gramo" and "Desde N g … c/u" become English. The catalog's own number formats stay (`$11.000`, `2,5 g`). Section 9 now says this, so nobody treats them as seller data.
4. **The concept now has a name.** "App text" is every word the app itself writes for a person to read, as opposed to seller data and the sheet's words. Criterion 1, the guard and the docs all rely on this line, so I added it to the glossary. I also consolidated the rule in a new capability file, `app-text.md`. Until the implementation updates each capability doc's quoted copy, that file wins over every "Rioplatense Spanish with vos" line and every quoted Spanish text in the other docs.
5. **The icon and text rule.** The "exact current word" kept as an accessible name becomes the English word. The rule's closed lists keep their members and only their words change. I wrote this into section 2 so it doesn't read as a contradiction.
6. **Glossary.** Three CONTEXT.md definitions name the Spanish seed or the empty-catalog message (Expense category, Catalog header and footer, Pinned catalog). I updated them in the glossary output. The `_In the app_` lines of every term change in the implementation's diff, as the PRD says.
7. **Baseline metric.** "From many to 0" now reads "0 at merge".
