## PM: consolidation

### Verdict

`approved`. The flow and the screens design are both validated, and no finding stops a job from being completed.

### PRD

#### Job to be done

**When** I open any screen, read a message the app or the API shows me, or read the catalog the bot posts, **I want** all of it in plain English, with every text kept in one place, **so I can** use the app without translating anything, and the product's owner can change any wording, or later add a language, in one place without touching the screens.

- **Protagonists.**
  - The people who read the app: the seller on the phone, their partner, and the customers who read the catalog in Telegram.
  - Zeta, who owns the wording.
- **What it refines.** Every capability's "run my business from my phone" job. It adds no capability, screen, route or step.
- **The pain today.** The app speaks only Spanish, its texts are scattered through the screens, and the design files, docs and tests are tied to exact Spanish wording.
- **Constraint that changes.** BRIEF.md's "Interface in Rioplatense Spanish; code and docs in English" becomes "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English". Each capability doc's "Rioplatense Spanish with vos" line changes the same way. `docs/product/app-text.md` overrides those lines until they are updated.
- **Assumption.** Existing sellers accept an English app until Zeta decides on translations.

**Success criteria**

1. **No Spanish app text where a person reads.** None in any of these:
   - every screen, toast, confirmation, and empty, loading and error state;
   - refusal messages and controls' accessible names;
   - the page title and the manifest;
   - the catalog text, a new business's default header and footer, and the bot's posts;
   - the operator commands' output.

   Seller data and the sheet's words are excluded.
2. **One place.** Every front text lives in one keyed module, and no screen writes its own literal.
3. **Nothing else changes.** Behaviour, flows, tap budgets, isolation, refusal codes and formats stay as they are. No route is in Spanish, and the old Spanish paths still redirect.
4. **The product docs agree.** The design docs, CONTEXT.md, `screens.json`, the tests and the e2e agree with the English app. No test is loosened.

**Left out.**
- Translations, a language switch or a translation library.
- Rewording beyond a faithful English version.
- English column titles for the import.
- A per-business language.
- Forcing a resend of the pinned message, or editing posts already sent.
- A migration of stored seeded names.
- File names (the app has no downloads).

#### Flow (validated by UX Lead)

Each job keeps its steps, screens, recovery paths, ending point and touch count. Only the words at each step change.

- **Get in and reach Inicio.**
  - Path: Entrar, then Inicio. Invitación and Reset reach Inicio the same way.
  - Wrong credentials, a dead or expired link, or being offline each show the same refusal, now in English, with the same recovery. The security texts reveal nothing more.
  - Old Spanish paths redirect as today.
  - The page title and the manifest's `lang` become `en`. This is not a step.
- **Record a sale.**
  - Path: Inicio → "+ Nueva venta" → Elegir cliente → saved, with the "Venta guardada" notice.
  - A refusal returns the same code, now with an English message, and the seller fixes the line and retries.
  - The tap budget is unchanged.
  - English may run longer. The 80- and 40-character limits still apply, and overflow moves behind More info.
- **Collect payment.**
  - Registrar cobro keeps its at-most-2-tap budget.
  - In the prototype it is reached from Inicio's and Clientes' debtor rows and from the customer's card.
  - Money keeps `$ 11.000`. Refusals and recovery are unchanged and worded in English.
- **Check and adjust stock.**
  - Reached from Inicio's low-stock rows or from Variedades → a variety's page → the stock entry form, Todas las entradas or Sacar del stock.
  - Grams keep `3,5 g` and dates keep `dd/mm`. Refusals and states are in English, and recovery is unchanged.
- **Catalog, import and Telegram.**
  - The catalog screen keeps its at-most-2-tap budget and its links to a variety's "En el catálogo", Integraciones and Mensaje del catálogo.
  - The pinned message is edited into English in place by the existing upkeep, at the next trigger or the next Argentina day change. Nothing new is posted.
  - The import is an operator script with no screen. Its codes and recovery (fix the file, run it again) are unchanged, and its report is in English.
- **Settings and business setup.**
  - Same steps, English texts.
  - A new business is seeded in English.
  - An existing business keeps its stored rows and may show Spanish categories, header or footer next to English screens until the seller renames them. They rename them through Categorías de gastos and Mensaje del catálogo, as today. Nothing prompts them.
- **Onboarding, Expenses, Análisis, Varieties and prices, Archived varieties.** Same steps, English words.
- **Across every job.** No screen, route, step or tap is added or removed. A visible literal not drawn from the keyed module is a defect that the gate's guard catches.

#### Screens (validated by UI Lead)

Every screen is the product screen as built: same structure, hierarchy, typography, colours, spacing, components, routes, tap budgets, refusal codes and formats (`$ 11.000`, `−$ 4.500`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, the catalog's `$11.000`). Only the visible text changes, and it comes in English from the keyed module.

- The `pantallas/*.dc.html` drawings are references for structure, not wording. A wording difference from a drawing is not a defect, and app-text.md wins.
- The registry's `section` labels stay as the design has them.
- Length limits hold in English: a help line or notice is at most 80 characters, and an empty-state title at most 40. Overflow moves behind More info.
- No icon is added or dropped.
- Every screen keeps the states it already has, each shown in English.

- **Getting in (Entrar, Invitación, NegocioNuevo, Reset).**
  - States: empty, filled, submitting, field error, brake, dead link, success.
  - The hint, the brake message and the dead-link texts reveal nothing more.
  - Reset is compared against Invitación's drawing as a structural reference. Its English follows the built Reset's own elements.
- **Inicio (phone, dark, computer).**
  - States: first run, filled, loading, offline and notices, pending cost.
  - "Today 07/10", English weekday and month names, and "and N g not yet costed", or "N g not yet costed" when fully pending.
  - Notices stay within 80 characters.
- **Nueva venta and Elegir cliente.** States: empty, filled, variety without price, picker empty, no result and filled, saving, error, offline. Refusal codes are unchanged.
- **Ventas and the sale's detail.** States: empty, loading, filled and error; paid, owing and gone.
- **Estados.** "Venta guardada", "Sin conexión", "That's gone", confirmations and the generic error, in English. Nothing new is revealed.
- **Clientes, the customer's card and Registrar cobro (phone and computer).** States: empty, filled, search with no result, owing or settled, and the payment form (filled, error, saving, saved).
- **Variedades and their screens** (a variety's page, Archivadas, the archived page, Nuevo precio, the stock entry form, Todas las entradas, Sacar del stock). States: empty, filled, loading, archived, form error, saving, confirmation. Pending cost reads "N g not yet costed".
- **Catálogo.**
  - States: filled, empty, loading, error.
  - The catalog's fixed words are in English.
  - The bot's empty catalog reads "Nothing to offer for now."
- **Configuración** (Negocio, Usuarios, Precios, Integraciones, Mensaje del catálogo, Categorías de gastos, Apariencia, Tu cuenta).
  - States: filled, empty lists, form error, saving, saved, destructive confirmations.
  - New-business seeds: the categories "Growing supplies", "Packaging", "Shipping and travel" and "Other"; the header "🌿 **CATALOG** 🌿"; the footer "📩 Questions and orders by private message.".
- **Onboarding, Gastos and the expense form, Análisis.** Each keeps its states. English weekday and month names and the pending marks apply.
- **Más, Ver todas, Nueva variedad.** Covered by the same words-only rule.
- **Outside the screens.**
  - The manifest, page title and document `lang` are `en`. The name stays "Apostrophe" and the `id` stays `"/"`.
  - The Telegram texts and the import report are in English.
  - No drawing is needed for these.
- **Stored seller data in Spanish** shows next to the English UI untouched. No screen hides or translates it.

#### Behaviour

1. **Scope.** Every word the app itself writes is English:
   - the front;
   - every API message, `detail` included;
   - the catalog text and the bot's posts;
   - the operator commands' output.

   It is a faithful version: each text keeps its facts (date, amount, grams, reason). Refusal codes, `contract.json`, ADR-0005 and ADR-0008 are unchanged. Routes stay English, and old Spanish paths redirect.
2. **Register.**
   - Plain, short, neutral and friendly; "you" where the Spanish used vos.
   - Buttons are imperative verbs.
   - Security texts reveal nothing more.
   - The icon and text rule's lists, 44 px targets and accessible-name rule hold, with the English word.
   - The length limits hold on the English copy.
3. **Terms.**
   - A concept label uses the glossary's English term. A sentence is translated faithfully.
   - CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines hold the English word, and the `_Avoid_` lists never forbid a shown word.
4. **Words inside formats.**
   - Weekday and month names, "Today", "since" and the pending marks are English.
   - Numbers and symbols stay as they are.
   - Sorting and search are unchanged, and Monday is the first day of the week.
5. **Seeds.** A new business is seeded in English. Stored rows are never rewritten. The import still matches and adds categories by name.
6. **Never translated.** Seller data, and the words the import reads from the sheet.
7. **Import.** It accepts the same sheet words. Its report and refusals are in English, and unfinished rows are quoted with the sheet's own text.
8. **Around the screens.** The title, the description, the document `lang` and the manifest `lang` are `en`. The name stays "Apostrophe", the `id` stays `"/"`, and installability passes.
9. **Telegram.**
   - The catalog text, every fixed word included, is built in English. Probarlo's and Buscar mi chat's reasons and the pin's statuses are English.
   - Posts already sent are untouched, and there is no per-business language.
   - Upkeep edits the pinned message into English in place.
   - A stored Spanish header and footer stay.
10. **One place for the texts.** One keyed module, with no library and no switch. `LIMITED_COPY`, `LONG_REFUSALS` and `SAMPLES` keep running, with nothing loosened.
11. **Guard in the gate.**
    - It fails on a screen's own visible literal, and on Spanish in the keyed module, the API messages or the catalog and bot texts.
    - It never flags seller data, the sheet's words, the kept formats or "Apostrophe".
    - It is fast and gives the same verdict on any date.
    - A real offender is fixed, never exempted.
    - Its mechanism is the Tech Lead's.
12. **Docs, design and tests.**
    - The design-system README says English is the app's language and that `pantallas/` are structure references.
    - `screens.json` steps follow the English app, while its `section` labels stay the design's.
    - The capability docs, CONTEXT.md, the tests and the e2e follow the English app. No test is loosened.

#### Metrics

There is no telemetry. Everything is read from tests and the gate.

- **No Spanish (criterion 1).** The guard finds 0 on the real tree. It is shown failing on an added Spanish message and passing on seller data, sheet words and formats.
- **One place (criterion 2).** 0 screens with their own literal. One edited entry changes every screen that uses it. 0 texts outside the module at merge.
- **Nothing else (criterion 3).**
  - `contract.json` and the formats are unchanged.
  - Every old Spanish path redirects.
  - No route in `screens.json` or the registry is Spanish.
  - The manifest has `lang` `en` and `id` `"/"`, and installability passes.
  - The title and the document language are English.
- **Telegram.** The catalog, its empty message included, is English. Upkeep edits a Spanish pin once, in place, with no new post.
- **Seeds.** A new business gets the English defaults, existing rows are untouched, and the import matching still works.
- **Limits.** The copy-limit tests pass on the English copy, and the PR lists every shortened text.
- **Docs (criterion 4).** Everything agrees, and the PR lists every edited test, none loosened.
- **Design.** `scripts/design/compare.sh` runs before and after, and the captures are judged on structure, not wording.

### Questions for the human

None.

### Observations on the design

- **Collect payment (flow).** The flow starts it "from the sale", but it is actually reached from the debtor rows and the customer's card.
- **Check and adjust stock (flow).** No "stock screen" exists. The path goes through Inicio's low-stock rows or Variedades and a variety's page.
- **Import (flow).** The flow names an "import screen", but the import is an operator script with no screen.
- **Flow coverage.** Onboarding, Expenses, Análisis, Varieties and prices and Archived varieties are not listed. Their words change too, and nothing in them is blocked.
- **Copy length.** Check by eye that English copy keeps within the 80- and 40-character limits on the phone screens.
- **`screens.json` steps.** They click and fill by exact Spanish text and must follow the English app. The `section` labels stay Spanish.
- **Reset.** The built Reset is its own smaller screen. Its English follows those elements, and the Invitación drawing is a structural reference only.
- **Inicio.** The design omits the setup-progress notice and the "includes pending cost" notes, which need English too.
- **Inicio fit.** Tabs, status words and bar labels must still fit on the phone width at 320 px and 130 % text.
- **Catálogo empty state.** The screen's own empty copy needs its own English, with a title of 40 characters or less, distinct from the bot's "Nothing to offer for now."
- **Unlisted screens.** Más, Ver todas, Nueva variedad and Inicio's first run are covered by the words-only rule.

## Interview: Tech Lead efímero→Tech Lead

### Round 1: questions

### Round 1: the whole app in English (GH-53)

I could not re-read the repository in this session, so these questions come from the engineering standards, the product knowledge and the ADRs. Check each one against the code before you answer.

**Where the plan fails today.** The gate guard is the weakest part. A guard that "fails on Spanish" in keyed modules, API messages, catalog text and bot texts has three problems:
- A word-list heuristic flags English words that are also Spanish ("pan", "real", "total", "no", "color") and misses short Spanish strings.
- Seller data, sheet words in `import adapters/layout.py` and cells, and "Apostrophe" must be exempt. Each exemption is a hole where Spanish can slip back in.
- English copy longer than the 80/40-char limits (LIMITED_COPY, LONG_REFUSALS, SAMPLES) breaks at runtime or in a design guard, not in the Spanish guard.

❓ **Q1** - **Guard design and where it runs**: How does the guard decide "Spanish" without a false alarm, and what does it scan? Spanish accents and ñ are not enough, because "Total" and "Stock" are valid in both languages. A word list needs an allowlist, and every allowlist entry is an exemption.
➡️ Don't detect language. Check structure instead:
- Every visible string lives in the one keyed module, which is a closed set of keys.
- A static scan (AST/regex) in tests/architecture flags string literals in screens, and in the backend message modules, that sit outside the keyed or message module.
- Spanish is caught only by a small stoplist of unambiguous Spanish words, plus accented characters, applied to the keyed module, API messages, `catalog_text` and the bot texts.
- Sheet words and seller data are exempt by path (layout.py), never by word.
The guard stays fast and date-independent because it parses files only and never touches the database.

❓ **Q2** - **Catalog text and the pinned Telegram message**: `catalog_text` is the single author (ADR-0022), and upkeep edits the pinned message in place through `needs_send` (ADR-0014). Once the text turns English, the existing pinned messages differ from what `catalog_text` now produces.
- Does the next 300 s ticker edit every live pinned message at once?
- What happens when the English text overflows Telegram's limit, or when `last_failure` is set?
- Do you rewrite stored header/footer rows? The PRD says stored rows are never migrated.
➡️ Make one test that renders old Spanish-text state through the new `catalog_text`. It must show that `needs_send` reports a change exactly once, that the edit stays in place with no new message, and that a too-long English render takes the same failure path as today. Don't touch stored rows. Change only the defaults for new businesses.

❓ **Q3** - **Existing tests, e2e and design guards tied to Spanish wording**: `muestra_guard`, `screens.json`, the design-system README, the e2e suite and the unit tests all quote Spanish copy.
- Do they get translated in the same subtask that translates the copy they cover?
- If the tests are rewritten together with the copy, they can no longer catch a lost key, a wrong key or a lost format.
➡️ Test through keys and `contract.json` wherever you can, and translate the literal expectations in the same subtask as the copy. Don't loosen any assertion to make the suite pass. Add one test that fails if a format (`$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`) or the pending mark "and N g not yet costed" changes.

❓ **Q4** - **Backend messages and ADR-0008**: The front never reads `detail`, and refusals are identified by code. Translating the `detail` strings should be safe, but ADR-0005 says no data goes in messages.
- Where does Spanish still live in the API: `detail` strings, operator CLI output, the import report?
- Could a translation accidentally change a refusal code, `contract.json`, or the text that import matching uses (the category names)?
➡️ Translate messages in place and leave codes and `contract.json` byte-identical. Add a contract-diff check to every backend subtask. Treat the import report and the CLI as separate surfaces, each with a test that fails on a stray Spanish string.

❓ **Q5** - **Subtask split and seams**: The standing sizing question. As I read the plan, the pieces are:
1. the front keyed module plus every screen migration;
2. the backend messages and the CLI;
3. catalog text, Telegram and seeds (BusinessOpening port);
4. docs, CONTEXT.md, README, `screens.json` and the design guards;
5. the gate guard.

Piece 1 is the risk: it touches every screen and probably carries well over four production modules, plus its tests and an ADR.
➡️ Split piece 1 first: (a) the keyed module and the guard seam, with no screens moved yet; (b) screens in groups, one per capability. The guard in (a) runs in report-only mode until the last screen group lands, then switches to fail. Piece 3 can run in parallel with the others, because it only meets the front through `contract.json`. Which seam lets two Developers work on different screen groups without both editing the keyed module?

### Invariants proposed
- No screen component defines a visible string literal; every text a person reads comes from the keyed front module.
- No subtask changes a refusal code or `contract.json`.
- The formats (`$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`) and the pending mark "and N g not yet costed" stay byte-identical in every diff.
- No subtask rewrites a stored seeded row; only the defaults for new businesses change.
- No existing test assertion is loosened or deleted to accommodate the English text; expectations are translated, not weakened.

### Round 1: answers

### Round 1 answers (GH-53)

What the code shows, which changes the shape of the work:
- **The front is where the Spanish is.** About 1,350 accented occurrences sit across 135 files in `web/src/screens/**`, tests included. There are also 16 copy modules (`*Messages.ts` / `messages.ts`) registered in `copyLimits.test.ts`, plus `api/refusals.ts`, `format/` and `index.html` (`lang="es"`).
- **The backend is nearly English already.** Domain `detail` strings are English (ADR-0008). The CLIs print English, and `spreadsheet_import/adapters/report.py` has no Spanish. The Spanish left in `app/` is:
  - the seeds: `expenses/domain/category.py:DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES` and `catalog/domain/header_footer.py:DEFAULT_HEADER/FOOTER`;
  - the catalog block words and `NOTHING_TO_OFFER` in `catalog/domain/text.py`;
  - sheet words: `spreadsheet_import/adapters/layout.py`, `cells.py`, `catalog/adapters/import_port.py:SHOWN_WORDS`, and the column labels `"Catálogo"`/`"Categoría"` inlined in `spreadsheet_import/domain/plan.py`;
  - the historical migrations 0003 and 0006.
- **Nothing compares a stored row with a default.** The defaults are read only by `catalog/adapters/opening.py` and `expenses/application/manage_categories.py`. Changing them affects new businesses only.

**Q1**: Accepted: structure first, plus a Spanish check over the places that hold app text. There are two guards and one shared marker list.

1. **Where the front's text lives.** The keyed module is the package `web/src/copy/`:
   - one entry point, `web/src/copy/index.ts`, typed with `as const`, so a missing key fails `tsc`;
   - one section file per capability, `web/src/copy/<area>.ts`. The existing 16 copy modules move there and keep `LIMITED_COPY`/`LONG_REFUSALS`, and `copyLimits.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_MODULES` follows them;
   - the code-to-text table of `api/refusals.ts`, and the words inside `format/` (weekday and month names, "Today", "since").

   Each word has exactly one entry; `messageSources.test.ts` keeps working on it. The PRD's "one keyed module" is about what can be seen: one entry per word, nothing in the screens. Being one file is not part of it.
2. **The front guard** is `web/src/architecture/appText.ts`, with `appText.real.test.ts` and fixture tests in vitest. The repository's fixtures are `.txt` files read with `?raw`, as `wallClock` does. It has two rules:
   - **No visible literal.** Over non-test `web/src/{screens,components,shell}/**/*.tsx`, it flags any JSX text node that contains a letter, and any letter-bearing string literal given to a closed list of visible props (`aria-label`, `title`, `placeholder`, `label`, `alt`, `ariaLabel`). Comments are blanked first, as `messageSources.blankComments` does.
     - Symbols with no letters pass: `‹`, `·`, `$`, `−`.
     - "Apostrophe" also lives in `copy/`, so the rule needs no word exemption.
   - **No Spanish in `web/src/copy/**`.** It flags any of `[áéíóúñü¿¡]`, or a whole-word, case-insensitive hit from a closed stoplist of unambiguous Spanish words (`de, del, la, las, los, el, que, para, por, con, sin, una, hoy, desde, tu, vos, ya, todavía, acá, borrar, guardá …`). Placeholders are blanked before the check.
3. **One marker list.** The stoplist and the character class live once, in `web/src/architecture/spanishMarkers.json`. Pytest reads that file from `web/`; vitest cannot read outside `web/` (ADR-0029).
4. **The backend guard** is `tests/architecture/test_app_text.py`. It is an AST walk over every `.py` in `app/**` and in the operator scripts (`scripts/drill.py`, `backup_copy.py`, `newest_backup.py`). It checks string constants and the literal parts of f-strings, and skips docstrings and comments.
   - **Exempt by path only, as a closed list** with a one-line reason each, and a test that fails when an entry no longer matches anything (the ADR-0043 pattern):
     - `app/migrations/**` (history, never rewritten);
     - the sheet-word files: `layout.py`, `cells.py`, `catalog/adapters/import_port.py`, and `spreadsheet_import/domain/rows.py`.
   - **`plan.py` loses its two inline column labels** to named constants in `rows.py`. That way the report-building domain stays scanned.
   - **Seller data is never a literal in code**, so it needs no rule.
5. **Fast and date-independent.** Both guards parse files only, touch no database and no clock. Each is shown failing on a deliberately added Spanish message, and passing on a sheet word and on a format.
6. **Where the Spanish check stops.** The length limits stay `copyLimits.test.ts`'s job; the Spanish guard does not try to measure them.

**Q2**: Accepted, with what the code adds.
- **No stored row changes.** Only `DEFAULT_HEADER/FOOTER`, `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES` and the words of `catalog_text` / `NOTHING_TO_OFFER` change. Migrations 0003 and 0006 keep their own Spanish copies, untouched.
- **The edit is not sent to every pin at once.** Each pinned message is edited at its business's next trigger. Otherwise the ticker reaches it when `sync_all_published` picks the business ("not yet checked today"), which is at the latest after the next Argentina day change. That is exactly the PRD's promise.
- **One test through `push(..., "upkeep")` with the fake Telegram:**
  - a pin whose `last_published_text` is a Spanish render gets exactly one edit, never a post;
  - a second push sends nothing;
  - a `last_failure` set is retried, as `needs_send` already does;
  - an English render over the limit takes today's `TooLong` path: status out of date, the triggering save untouched.
- **Probarlo's test send uses the same text**, so the existing test that the test copy equals the published copy covers it.
- **The design sample keeps its Spanish names.** `scripts/design/sample_app.py` opens a new business, which would now get English categories, header and footer, and that would break `muestra.md` and its guard against the Spanish `pantallas/`. So the sample sets its categories, header and footer explicitly through the API to the names `pantallas/` draws. Those are seller data, so this is legitimate. `muestra.md` stays byte-identical and the muestra guard is unchanged.

**Q3**: Partly overruled.
- **Behaviour tests keep asserting the literal English a person sees**, by role and name, never through keys (CODING_STANDARDS §5 and §10: tests go through what the user sees). If a test read the key the screen reads, it would pass on a wrong key.
- **Expectations are translated one for one, in the same subtask as the copy they cover.** No assertion is dropped, loosened or turned into a regex, and the PR lists every edited test.
- **Losing a key fails `tsc`**, because the module is typed `as const`.
- **Accepted: one format-lock test** in `web/src/format/`, asserting `$ 11.000`, `−$ 4.500`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`, "and N g not yet costed" and "N g not yet costed". The backend twin pins the catalog's `$11.000` and `2,5 g`.
- **What stays Spanish:**
  - `pantallas/` and `muestra.md` (the design reference for structure, not app text);
  - the static registry checks that read design files (`sectionOccurrences`).
- **A risk I am naming: the section crop.** If `comparisons.mjs` crops both sides by one `section` label, as ADR-0042's GH-224 amendment reads, an English built page and a Spanish design no longer share it. `screens.json` entries then gain an optional built-side label, `section_built`, and step `click`/`press`/`wait` texts become the English the built app shows. This is design tooling, with the two registry tests as its seams.
- **e2e specs are translated by the subtask that changes the screens they read.**

**Q4**: Accepted in part, and narrower than the question assumes. The API's `detail` strings, the CLIs' output and the import report are already English.
- **What is left on the backend** is the seeds, the catalog text and the sheet words. The sheet words stay Spanish by design.
- **`contract.json` is never edited in this task.** `tests/test_refusal_codes.py` already pins codes and kinds, and the invariant below makes "no diff to `contract.json`" explicit.
- **The refusal *texts* are front copy.** They move from `api/refusals.ts` into the sections, keyed by code, with `Record<RefusalCode, …>` still exhaustive.
- **Import category matching is untouched.** It matches by name against stored rows, and stored rows don't change.
- **Overruled: no separate test per surface.** The one AST guard covers the CLIs, the report and the catalog text by construction. Each surface keeps its existing tests, with their expectations translated.

**Q5**: The split is accepted. The report-only mode is overruled: a guard that passes everything until the end proves nothing while the work is under way. I use instead the repository's ratchet (ADR-0037).
1. **S1, the front foundation:**
   - creates `web/src/copy/` with `index.ts` already re-exporting one empty section file per area, so later subtasks never edit `index.ts`;
   - moves the 16 copy modules and `refusals.ts`'s texts into their sections, with no wording change;
   - creates a `common` section (Close, Save, "No connection", "That's gone", the retry) in English, frozen after S1;
   - translates the words inside `format/` and the pending mark, with the format-lock test and the test lines those words break;
   - sets `index.html` `lang="en"`, the title, and the manifest's description and `lang`;
   - adds the marker list and the front guard. The guard fails from day one on new offenders, and holds a shrink-only `SPANISH_LEFT` list of today's files, keyed by path.
2. **S2, the backend** (after S1, for the marker list; in parallel with the screen groups): seeds, `catalog_text`, the `plan.py` constants, the backend guard and the upkeep test. It touches no front file.
3. **S3 to S6, the screen groups**, in parallel after S1. Each owns its section file(s) under `copy/`, its screens, their tests, the e2e lines that read them and its capability docs' quoted copy, and removes its paths from `SPANISH_LEFT`. Two developers never share a file.
   - (a) getting in, Estados, the shell and Configuración;
   - (b) sales, customers and payments;
   - (c) varieties, stock, withdrawals, the catalog screen and pinned-catalog status;
   - (d) Inicio, Análisis, Gastos and the Onboarding.
4. **S7, the close:**
   - CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines, BRIEF.md, the design-system README and `app-text.md`'s precedence note;
   - the labels in `screens.json` (plus the registry tests within their seams);
   - asserting `SPANISH_LEFT` is empty and deleting it, so no exemption survives the task's merge;
   - the design captures.

**Invariants**: all five accepted, plus four more:
- `web/src/api/contract.json` has no diff.
- `app/migrations/**` has no diff.
- `SPANISH_LEFT` only shrinks, and is empty and deleted before the task merges.
- No file under `app/` or `web/src/` is exempt from either guard except by the closed path list in Q1.

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2: guard strength, parallel conflicts and the design tooling

Your answers settle the backend scope, the seeds and the upkeep test, so I won't ask about those again. Each question below is a place where the plan as you wrote it still fails.

**Where the plan fails now.** The front guard's Spanish rule will pass almost every real Spanish button label. The stoplist holds function words (`de, la, para, con …`) and accented characters. Most labels are single unaccented words: "Ventas", "Clientes", "Guardar", "Cancelar", "Stock". None of them trips the rule. Once a file leaves `SPANISH_LEFT`, nothing proves it is actually translated.

❓ **Q1** - **A guard that cannot see "Guardar"**: Spanish is checked with a stoplist and a character class, and the guard is the only evidence that the app is "all in English". It misses exactly the strings people read most. The guard also has a second weak point: the ratchet works per file, so a half-translated file stays listed, and a file that is removed from the list is then checked only by the weak rule.
➡️ Make the ratchet work per key, not per file.
- S1 snapshots the baseline: `copy-baseline.json`, mapping each key path to its pre-change Spanish value, taken from the base commit.
- The guard fails when a key's current value equals its baseline value and the key is not on an explicit `SAME_IN_BOTH` list (`Total`, `Stock` …, each with a reason).
- The guard also fails on any key that is in neither the baseline nor `copy/`.
- The stoplist stays as a second net for new strings.
- Do the same on the backend for the seeds and the `catalog_text` words. A constants snapshot from the base commit is enough there.

The end state then reads "no key still carries its Spanish baseline", which is checkable, instead of "no stopword found".

❓ **Q2** - **Shared files that break "two developers never share a file"**: S3 to S6 run in parallel, and each one removes its own paths from `SPANISH_LEFT`. They all edit the same file, so every merge conflicts. The `common` section has a second problem. It is frozen after S1 with a guessed list of words. Group (b) will need "Remove" or "Undo", and group (c) will need the same word. If each group adds its own copy, "each word exactly one entry" fails and `messageSources` complains. If neither may add it, the group is blocked.
➡️ Split the ledger: one `spanish-left/<group>.json` per group, created in S1. S7 deletes the directory. For `common`, either:
- let S1 harvest every word that two or more of today's copy modules share, or
- allow additive-only edits to `common` through a file per group (`common.<group>.ts`) that S7 merges.

Which do you pick? Say how a Developer in S4 learns that a word already exists in S5's section.

❓ **Q3** - **Escape routes in the "no visible literal" rule**: The rule covers JSX text and a closed list of props. Spanish text can still reach the screen through:
- a string held in a variable or a `const LABELS = {…}` inside a screen;
- a ternary such as `{busy ? 'Guardando' : 'Guardar'}`;
- a template literal;
- a helper that returns text;
- `aria-describedby`/`aria-description`, `document.title`, `window.confirm`;
- dates and numbers: `Intl.DateTimeFormat('es-AR')` or `toLocaleDateString` produces Spanish weekday and month names, even if `format/` is "translated".

You say the formats stay unchanged (`$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm`). So the locale for numbers stays `es-AR`. How do you keep names of days and months English then?
➡️ Widen the rule from a prop list to a type rule. In screens, components and the shell, any string literal with a letter fails unless it is one of:
- an import path;
- a CSS class or `data-*` value;
- a key given to the `copy` accessor;
- the member of a small closed set of non-visible props.

Add an architecture test that bans `toLocale*`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat` and `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` outside `format/`. Inside `format/`, month and weekday names come from `copy`, not from the locale.

❓ **Q4** - **`muestra.md` byte-identical against an English app**: You keep `muestra.md`, `pantallas/` and the muestra guard Spanish by setting the seller data explicitly. But the muestra guard is keyed on copy. If `muestra.md` or `pantallas/` contain chrome text (nav labels, buttons, section titles, "Hoy"), the built English page can no longer match them. The seller-data trick covers only what a seller types. And ADR-0042's crop by one `section` label breaks, as you said. A new `section_built` label makes `screens.json` carry two names per section.
➡️ Before S7, list which strings in `muestra.md` and `pantallas/` the app writes (chrome) and which the seller wrote (data). If any chrome is Spanish, either the muestra guard compares structure only (roles and order, not text), or `muestra.md` is regenerated in English from the same sample. State which one, and which ADR it amends.

❓ **Q5** - **S1 is the biggest piece**: S1 does all of these:
- creates `copy/` and moves 16 modules plus `refusals.ts`;
- translates `format/`, `common`, `index.html` and the manifest;
- adds the format-lock test;
- adds the marker list, the front guard and the ledger.

That is more than four production modules, plus its tests and an ADR (the guard's ADR), and every other subtask waits for it. Anything that goes wrong here stalls the whole task.
➡️ Split S1 in two. S1a is the pure move, with the `copy/` skeleton and no wording change, so the diff is mechanical and reviewable. S1b adds the guards, the baseline, `common`, `format/` and the `index.html`/manifest change. S2 (backend) can start in parallel with S1a, because the backend marker list is a separate file. Do you accept? If not, which part is least risky to leave inside S1?

### Invariants proposed
- A screen-group subtask's diff touches only the `copy/` files and the ledger file it owns, never another group's.
- No file under `web/src/screens`, `components` or `shell` calls `toLocale*`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat` or `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`; day and month names come only from `copy`.
- No screen, component or shell file holds a visible string literal in a variable, ternary, template or helper; every visible text is read from `copy`.

### Round 2: answers

### Round 2 answers (GH-53)

Two things in the code change two of my round-1 answers:
- **The design crop and the muestra guard never read the built app's words.** `scripts/design/comparisons.mjs:384` crops by the `section` label on the **design** page only (`designOf(s).dc.html`). `tests/design/muestra_guard.py` reads `pantallas/`. `scripts/design/capture_muestra.py` writes its own words (lines 196-202 and 376-382) around figures it reads from the API. So `section_built` is withdrawn: nothing needs it.
- **Dates are never formatted by `Intl`.** `web/src/format/dates.ts` builds them by hand, and the only `Intl` in `web/src` is `format/money.ts`'s three `Intl.NumberFormat('es-AR')`, which give the kept formats `$ 11.000` and `3,5 g`.

**Q1**: Accepted: the ratchet works per key, and the stoplist stays as a second net. How it is represented:
1. **The baseline.** `web/src/architecture/fixtures/copyBaseline.es.json` maps each key path to its Spanish value.
   - It is generated once from S1a's head. S1a is a pure move, so its values are exactly the base commit's, now keyed.
   - It is never edited by hand afterwards.
   - It sits outside `copy/`, so the Spanish rule does not scan it.
   - It stays after the merge, so a revert to a Spanish value fails forever.
2. **The rule over a section.** It applies to every section file that is not pending (see Q2), and fails a key when either:
   - its value equals its baseline value, unless the key is in that section's own exported `SAME_IN_BOTH` list (each entry with a reason, e.g. `Stock`, `Total`, `Packaging`, `VIP`);
   - its value trips the marker list.

   A key absent from the baseline is a new string, and only the marker list checks it. A key in the baseline that is no longer in `copy/` is allowed: its text is gone, and the visible-literal rule makes sure it did not move into a screen.
3. **The backend equivalent.** It is a pinned, hand-written list in `tests/architecture/test_app_text.py`, not a generated snapshot: S2 is the only place these strings change, and the list is short. Each of these constants must differ from its old Spanish value:
   - `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES`;
   - `DEFAULT_HEADER` and `DEFAULT_FOOTER`;
   - `NOTHING_TO_OFFER`;
   - the four fixed words of `catalog_text`'s blocks.

   Those values are also asserted in English through the catalog's reference-set test.
4. **The end state.** It reads: "no section is pending, and no key carries its baseline value outside `SAME_IN_BOTH`". That can be checked.

**Q2**: I take neither option as written. No shared file exists, ledger included.
- **The pending marker lives inside each section file.** S1b adds `export const PENDING = true` to every section file. The group that owns a section translates it, then deletes the line; only then do the baseline and marker rules apply to that section. S7 asserts that no section exports `PENDING`, and removes the mechanism.
- **The screen-literal allowance is one file per group:** `web/src/architecture/textLeft/<group>.json`, holding that group's screen paths. S1b creates them and each group empties its own. S7 asserts all are empty and deletes the directory.
- **`common`.** S1a harvests into `common` every value that appears in two or more of today's copy modules or `refusals.ts`, still in Spanish. It is a pure dedup with no visible change, and `messageSources` covers it. `common` is translated by S1b and frozen after that.
- **A word needed by two groups.** A word that is not shared today stays in its section. A group that needs a word owned by another group's section imports that key read-only, and never copies it. That is how "each message has exactly one entry" holds.
  - A Developer in S4 finds the word in S5's section with a grep of `web/src/copy/` on the Spanish value. Until S5 lands, the Spanish values are still there, and the baseline JSON lists them all.
  - Two groups that independently write the same English word for two *different* Spanish messages are not a duplicate: they are two messages. `messageSources` lists only messages that are deliberately shared.

**Q3**: Accepted: the literal rule becomes a type rule over the TypeScript AST.
- **The parse.** `ts.createSourceFile`, from the `typescript` package already installed for `tsc`, inside vitest. A regex cannot tell a comparison from a label.
- **What it scans.** Non-test `web/src/{screens,components,shell}/**/*.{ts,tsx}`.
- **What fails.** Any string literal, template literal part or JSX text with a letter, unless it sits in one of these contexts:
  - an import or export specifier;
  - a type position;
  - an operand of `===`/`!==`, or a `case` label (comparisons with API enums such as `'owes'`);
  - an object property name;
  - an element-access argument;
  - the `className` attribute, or an argument to the class helper;
  - a `data-*` attribute;
  - a closed list of non-visible props (`type`, `role`, `id`, `name`, `htmlFor`, `inputMode`, `enterKeyHint`, `autoComplete`, `variant`, `action`, `icon`, `nombre`, `to`, `href`, `textOnly`, `kind`);
  - an argument to the `copy` accessor.

  Variables, ternaries, templates, helpers, `aria-description`/`aria-describedby` text, `document.title` and `window.confirm` are therefore all covered.
- **False alarms.** One found on the real tree is fixed by moving the value into a named constant in a non-scanned module, or by adding a context to the closed list with its reason in the same diff. It is never fixed by a per-file exemption.
- **The locale ban.** Accepted: an architecture test bans `toLocale*`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat` and `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` everywhere in non-test `web/src`, `format/` included. `Intl.NumberFormat('es-AR')` stays, only in `format/money.ts`: it is what keeps `$ 11.000` and `3,5 g` byte-identical. Every weekday and month name, "Today" and "since" come from `copy` through `format/`.

All three proposed invariants are accepted, with the ban written as "anywhere in `web/src`" rather than only in screens, components and the shell.

**Q4**: Settled from the code, with no regeneration and no change to the muestra guard.
- **Nothing in the muestra chain reads the built app.**
  - `muestra_guard` compares `pantallas/` (the design files) with `muestra.md`.
  - `muestra.md` is rendered by `capture_muestra.py`, which writes its own words around API figures.
  - The section crop runs on the design page only (`comparisons.mjs:384`).
- **What `pantallas/` is.** It is the design reference for structure, and the README change says so. Its chrome stays Spanish, and the built side of a capture shows English, judged by eye.
- **What does change in the design tooling.** The built-side steps of `screens.json` (`click`/`press`/`wait`) become the English the app shows, in S7, with the two registry tests as seams.
- **The sample's data stays as `pantallas/` draws it.** `sample_app.py` sets the sample's categories, header and footer to what `pantallas/` draws. That keeps `muestra.md` byte-identical, and so `test_muestra_seed` passes; this matters because new businesses are now seeded in English.
- **No ADR is amended for this.** The guard ADR written in S1b records it.

**Q5**: Accepted, with one ordering change.
- **S1a** is the pure, mechanical move, with no wording change:
  - the `copy/` skeleton: `index.ts` and one section file per area, each present from the start;
  - the 16 copy modules and `refusals.ts`'s texts moved into their sections;
  - the `common` harvest;
  - `KNOWN_MODULES` updated;
  - the baseline JSON generated.
- **S2** (backend) starts in parallel with S1a, and creates the one marker list, `web/src/architecture/spanishMarkers.json`, which pytest reads.
  - I keep one list rather than two copies: two copies would drift.
  - S2 owns no other file under `web/`.
- **S1b** runs after S1a and S2, and holds:
  - both front rules and the `PENDING` markers;
  - the `textLeft/<group>.json` files;
  - the locale ban;
  - `common` and `format/` in English, with the format-lock test and the test lines those words break;
  - `index.html` and the manifest;
  - the guard's ADR.
- **What follows.** S3 to S6 (the screen groups) run in parallel after S1b, then S7.

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3: loopholes in the ratchet, shared terms, scan scope and routes

Your answers settle the withdrawal of `section_built`, the locale ban, the type rule, the muestra chain and the S1a/S2/S1b order. I won't ask about those again.

**Where the plan fails now.** The per-key baseline can be beaten by renaming a key. A Developer who moves `guardar` to `saveButton` and keeps "Guardar" makes the key absent from the baseline. By your own rule it is then "a new string" checked only by the marker list, and "Guardar" passes the marker list. The ratchet's end state, "no key carries its baseline value", becomes false in practice.

❓ **Q1** - **Key rename defeats the baseline**: Keys are the lookup, but the thing that must not survive is the Spanish value.
➡️ Compare values, not keys.
- A section that is not pending fails when any of its values equals any value in `copyBaseline.es.json`, wherever that value sat before. The exception is a `SAME_IN_BOTH` entry.
- For parametrised entries (functions), compare the rendered output on a fixed argument set, or compare the template parts.
- Keep the marker list as a second net.
- Write one fixture test that renames a key and keeps its Spanish value, and shows the guard failing.

❓ **Q2** - **Four groups translate the same concept four ways**: S3 to S6 run in parallel and each translates its own screens. The English for glossary concepts is a decision, not a translation. "Fiado", "Probarlo", "Estados", a pending cost, "Retiros", "Variedad" and the "owed" balance each have several English words. CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines are only updated in S7, after all four groups have chosen. Four Developers each decide alone, and each review sees one branch. The result is "Tab" on one screen, "Credit" on another and "Owed" in the Telegram text.
➡️ Move the terminology decision before the groups. S1b (or a small S1c) updates CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines in English, one per glossary concept, and reviews the list with the PM or the human. Groups translate to those lines. Add a test that each `_In the app_` term appears in `copy/` as written, so a group cannot drift. Who approves the English terms, and where is the list recorded before S3 starts?

❓ **Q3** - **S1b rewrites tests in every group**: S1b puts `common` and `format/` into English while every screen is still Spanish. "Close", "Save", "No connection", weekday names and "Today" appear in tests, e2e specs and accessible names across all four groups. You say S1b also fixes "the test lines those words break". That means S1b edits files that S3 to S6 own, so the "two developers never share a file" rule fails for those files. It also makes S1b large: both guards, the ledgers, the locale ban, `common`, `format/`, `index.html`, the manifest, the ADR and a cross-group test edit. And the app is half English between S1b and the last group.
➡️ Split S1b in two.
- S1b-1: the guards, `PENDING` markers, ledgers, locale ban and ADR. It changes no wording.
- S1b-2: translate `common` and `format/` and fix the dependent test lines across all groups. It runs when no group branch is open, or the groups wait for it, and nothing else lands at the same time.
Do you accept? If you keep it as one piece, say how a group's branch survives a rebase over S1b's test edits in its own files.

❓ **Q4** - **Scan scope and shared components**: The type rule scans `screens`, `components` and `shell`. Visible text can also come from other directories under `web/src` (hooks, state, `lib`, toasts, error mapping, `routes`, `api/*`). You scan none of them. Also, `textLeft/<group>.json` lists screen paths per group, but `components/**` is shared by all groups, and nobody owns it. When S4 translates a shared component, S5 and S6 branches may be using it too.
➡️ Scan all non-test `web/src` except `copy/`, `architecture/`, `api/contract.json` and `format/money.ts`, with the same contexts. Assign each shared component to exactly one group or to S1b-2, and list the owners in the ledger names, so every path is owned once. If a component has text and two groups use it, it moves to `copy/common` in S1a or S1b-2, not in a group.

❓ **Q5** - **Routes**: The task title says "routes and every text a person reads", and your answers never mention routes. If the URL paths are Spanish (`/ventas`, `/clientes`…), several pieces change at once:
- the path table the screens and links use;
- the manifest `start_url`;
- the service worker cache and fallback;
- the backend SPA fallback list;
- the links the Telegram bot posts to the app;
- bookmarks and installed PWAs, which hold the old paths.
Which of these exist in the code, and who owns each change? If the old paths must still work, where do the redirects live?
➡️ Treat routes as their own subtask or a part of S1a. Put the paths in one routes module that every link, navigation call, the manifest and the tests import. Add an architecture test that bans a literal path string anywhere else. Add a redirect table from the old paths to the new ones, with a test for each. If the PRD says old links need not keep working, ask the PM to confirm that in writing before you drop the redirects.

### Invariants proposed
- No section's copy value outside `SAME_IN_BOTH` equals a Spanish value in `copyBaseline.es.json`, whatever its key.
- Every English label of a glossary concept equals that concept's `_In the app_` line in CONTEXT.md.
- No file outside the routes module holds a literal route path string.

### Round 3: answers

### Round 3 answers (GH-53)

**Q1**: Accepted. The ratchet compares values, wherever they sit.
- **What the baseline holds.** `copyBaseline.es.json` keeps the set of Spanish *values*. The keys stay only for readability.
  - A function entry is stored as its output rendered with `SAMPLES` (`web/src/copy/limits.ts`), the same fixed arguments `copyLimits.test.ts` already uses.
- **The rule.** A section that is not pending fails when any of its values, rendered the same way, equals any baseline value, whatever its key.
  - The comparison is after NFC, trimmed and case-folded, so "guardar" and "Guardar" both fail.
- **The one exception.** `SAME_IN_BOTH` becomes a list of *values*, each with a reason (e.g. `Stock`, `Total`, `Packaging`, `VIP`), exported by the section that uses it.
- **The second net.** The marker list stays.
- **The proof.** One fixture test renames a key, keeps "Guardar", and shows the guard failing. A second test does the same through a function entry.

**Q2**: Accepted: terminology is fixed before any group opens. This needs no PM: the PRD settles it.
- **What the PRD says.** "A label that names a concept uses the glossary's English term" (`app-text.md`, Terms), and the `_In the app_` line "holds the English word the seller sees". So each `_In the app_` line is the concept's English glossary term (the headword: Balance, Credit, Payment, Stock withdrawal …), and the English word for each pricing rule.
  - Proposing a word, as the PRD allows, applies only where a `_In the app_` line names something with no headword. The tab names are an example: Home, More, Settings.
- **Where it lands, and who approves.** S1b-2 (Q3) writes every `_In the app_` line in CONTEXT.md. Terms with "no word of its own", or "operator terminal only", keep saying so. The reviewer of S1b-2 approves the lines against the headwords.
  - From then on, CONTEXT.md is the recorded list, and no group may edit it.
  - Anything left open goes to PM through S1b-2's review, not a group's.
- **The test.** A pytest parses CONTEXT.md. For every `_In the app_` line that names a word, it asserts that word appears verbatim in at least one value under `web/src/copy/`.
  - It runs once the last section is no longer pending. S7 turns it on, and asserts that no section exports `PENDING`.
  - Before that, a group drifting from the list is caught in review, since each brief names CONTEXT.md as the source of its words.
- **The invariant**, accepted as: "Every label that names a glossary concept uses that concept's `_In the app_` word."

**Q3**: Accepted: S1b is split, and the groups wait for the second half.
- **S1b-1** changes no wording:
  - both front rules, the `PENDING` markers, the `textLeft/<group>.json` ledgers, the locale ban and the guard's ADR.
- **S1b-2** changes the shared wording:
  - CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines (Q2);
  - `common`, the words inside `format/`, the format-lock test, `index.html` and the manifest;
  - every shared component and the shell (Q4);
  - every test line and e2e line those words break, in any group's files.
- **Order.** S1a and S2 (in parallel), then S1b-1, then S1b-2, then S3 to S6 in parallel, then S7. No group branch exists while S1b-2 runs, so nothing is rebased over it.
- **The half-English app.** Subtasks land on the task branch, and the task merges to `master` once, after S7. The half-English state is never on `master` and no seller sees it.

**Q4**: Accepted, with what the code adds.
- **The scope widens** to all non-test `web/src`, except:
  - `copy/`;
  - `architecture/` and `test/`;
  - `design/` (registry tooling);
  - `routes.ts`, which holds paths, not text; its own scan stays (Q5).

  One context is added to the closed list: the first argument of `Intl.NumberFormat`, which keeps `format/money.ts`'s `'es-AR'` without exempting the file. `api/contract.json` holds codes, not text; it is never translated and the text rule does not scan it.
- **Ownership: every scanned path has exactly one owner.**
  - S1b-2 owns everything shared: `components/**`, `shell/**`, `flash/**`, `format/**`, and the text-bearing parts of `api/` (the refusal table's texts are already in sections after S1a), `domain/`, `stock/` and `sales/`.
  - S3 to S6 own only `screens/**`, each group by a fixed list of paths written into its ledger by S1b-1.
- **A pytest proves the ownership.** It asserts the ledgers are pairwise disjoint, and that every scanned file with an offending literal is in exactly one ledger. A component with text used by two groups is therefore never a group's: it is S1b-2's, and its words go to `common`.

**Q5**: Routes are already English and owned elsewhere. Nothing is renamed and nothing goes to PM.
- **The PRD settles it**: "Routes are already English. This change only checks that none is left in Spanish."
- **What the code has:**
  - **One route module.** `web/src/routes.ts` is the one place a path is written, and `routes.test.ts` already fails on any route literal elsewhere in `web/src` or `web/e2e`. Your third invariant already holds.
  - **The redirects.** They live once, server side, in `app/legacy_front_paths.json`, served by `app/static_spa.py` (ADR-0024 amendment, ADR-0033). Each row is pinned old-to-target by `tests/fixtures/legacy_front_paths.json` and tested in `tests/test_serving.py`. The front has no redirects.
  - **The manifest's `start_url` and `scope` are `/`.** Only `lang` changes, `es` to `en`, in S1b-2.
  - **`web/public/sw.js` caches nothing** (accounts.md).
  - **The bot posts no link to the app.** The catalog text has none.
- **What S7 adds, the check the PRD asks for:**
  - a test that no path segment in `routes.ts` or `scripts/design/screens.json` equals a segment of any `old` path in `app/legacy_front_paths.json`;
  - that every path is ASCII.
- **What stays untouched:** the redirect table and its tests.

### Closing

### Closing summary (round 4 of 4, so I close with what I have)

**The plan still fails in one place.** S1b-2 is too big. It writes the CONTEXT.md `_In the app_` lines, translates `common` and `format/`, and covers `index.html`, the manifest, every shared component and the shell. It also fixes every dependent test and e2e line in all four groups. That is well over four production modules plus tests, and the groups wait on it. If it runs out of turns, S3 to S6 are blocked. I would rather you split it now, so no piece depends on one Developer finishing everything. These cuts would work:
- S1b-2a: CONTEXT.md terms, `common` and `format/`, with the tests they break.
- S1b-2b: shared components, the shell and `index.html`/manifest.

**What was decided:**
- **Where the text lives.** All front text moves into the `web/src/copy/` package, one section per area. A word has one entry, typed `as const`. Routes are already English and the redirect table is untouched. The backend keeps its codes and `contract.json`. Only the seeds, the `catalog_text` words and the `plan.py` constants change. Stored rows and migrations are never touched.
- **The guards.**
  - The front has a type rule over the TypeScript AST on all non-test `web/src`, plus a locale ban. `Intl.NumberFormat('es-AR')` stays only in `format/money.ts`.
  - The Spanish check compares values with the baseline `copyBaseline.es.json` after normalisation (NFC, trim, case-fold). It also uses one shared marker list.
  - The backend has an AST guard with a closed exempt-path list, plus pinned English constants.
  - A pytest proves that every scanned path has exactly one owner.
- **Terms.** CONTEXT.md's `_In the app_` lines are written once, in S1b-2, before the groups open. The glossary's English headword is the term. Groups do not edit it.
- **Tests.** Behaviour tests assert the literal English a person sees. Every expectation is translated one for one, and none is loosened. A format-lock test pins `$ 11.000`, `3,5 g`, `dd/mm` and the pending mark.
- **Telegram upkeep.** One test through `push(..., "upkeep")` covers an old Spanish pin getting one in-place edit, no second send, the `last_failure` retry, and the `TooLong` path.
- **Design.** `muestra.md` stays byte-identical. The sample's seller data stays as `pantallas/` draws it. Only the built-side steps in `screens.json` become English.
- **Order.** S1a and S2 run in parallel, then S1b-1, then S1b-2, then S3 to S6 in parallel, then S7. The half-English state never reaches `master`, because the task merges once after S7.

**Known risks that stay open:**
- **Single-word Spanish.** The marker list cannot see an unaccented Spanish word that is not in the baseline. A genuinely new Spanish string such as "Ventas" in new copy would pass the marker check. Only the baseline comparison and review catch it.
- **`SAME_IN_BOTH`.** It is a hole by design. Each entry needs a reason that a reviewer reads, and nothing else polices it.
- **Terminology drift.** The test that checks `_In the app_` words against `copy/` only turns on at S7. Until then, groups are held to the glossary by review alone.
- **Backend text guard.** Outside the pinned constants, the backend guard relies on the same marker list. "The backend is already English" rests on one read of the code, not on a dictionary check.
- **English layout.** English strings may be longer. `copyLimits` checks the character limits, but whether the layout holds at narrow widths is judged by eye in the design captures.
- **Grammar.** Plurals and interpolated entries ("1 customers") are not covered by any rule. Only the `SAMPLES` render in the tests sees them.

### Invariants proposed
- No screen-group subtask edits CONTEXT.md, the `common` section or a shared component.

## Tech Lead

### What this task builds

The whole app becomes English, and every text the front shows lives in one keyed package. The plan follows the four interview rounds, with one change: the closing's split of S1b-2 is **accepted**. The shared rewording runs in two pieces, so no single Developer holds all four screen groups back.

### Shape

- **Front text: `web/src/copy/`.**
  - `index.ts` re-exports one section file per area (`common`, `auth`, `settings`, `sales`, `customers`, `varieties`, `stock`, `catalog`, `home`, `analysis`, `expenses`, `onboarding`, `refusals`, `format`, `shell`, `states`).
  - Every entry is `as const`. A parametrised entry is a function.
  - The 16 existing copy modules and the texts of `api/refusals.ts` move here. `refusals.ts` keeps `Record<RefusalCode, …>` exhaustive and reads its text from `copy/refusals.ts`.
  - `LIMITED_COPY`/`LONG_REFUSALS` stay per section. `copyLimits.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_MODULES` lists the sections.
  - `SAMPLES.name` becomes an English 24-character name; the limit is still counted the same way.
- **Backend text.** Already English except for these, which change:
  - the seeds `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES`, `DEFAULT_HEADER` and `DEFAULT_FOOTER`;
  - `catalog_text`'s fixed block words (`• N% indica / M% sativa`, `• Aroma/flavour: …`, `• Price per gram: $X`, `• From N g: $Y each`);
  - `NOTHING_TO_OFFER`.

  Sheet words stay Spanish. The `"Catálogo"`/`"Categoría"` labels inlined in `spreadsheet_import/domain/plan.py` become named constants in `domain/rows.py`. Nothing changes in `contract.json`, migrations or stored rows.
- **Routes** are already English. The redirect table (`app/legacy_front_paths.json`) and its tests are untouched. S7 adds the check that no route is Spanish.

### Guards (S1b-1, new ADR-0048 "App text lives in one package and two guards keep it there")

1. **Visible-literal rule** (`web/src/architecture/appText.ts`, vitest).
   - It parses every non-test `.ts`/`.tsx` under `web/src` with `ts.createSourceFile`, except `copy/`, `architecture/`, `test/`, `design/` and `routes.ts`.
   - It flags every string literal, template part or JSX text that contains a letter, except in a closed list of contexts:
     - import or export specifiers;
     - type positions;
     - operands of `===`/`!==` and `case` labels;
     - property names and element-access arguments;
     - `className` and the class helper's arguments;
     - `data-*` attributes;
     - the closed list of non-visible props (`type`, `role`, `id`, `name`, `htmlFor`, `inputMode`, `enterKeyHint`, `autoComplete`, `variant`, `action`, `icon`, `nombre`, `to`, `href`, `textOnly`, `kind`);
     - the first argument of `Intl.NumberFormat`.
   - The allowance is one ledger per owner, `web/src/architecture/textLeft/<owner>.json`. A pytest asserts the ledgers are pairwise disjoint and that every offending file is in exactly one.
2. **Locale ban.** No `toLocale*`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat` or `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` anywhere in non-test `web/src`.
3. **Spanish rule over `copy/`.** It applies to every section that does not export `PENDING = true`. Each value is rendered with `SAMPLES` for functions, then NFC-normalised, trimmed and case-folded. It fails when either:
   - it equals any value of `web/src/architecture/fixtures/copyBaseline.es.json`, whatever its key, unless the section's own `SAME_IN_BOTH` lists that value with a reason;
   - it trips `web/src/architecture/spanishMarkers.json` (`[áéíóúñü¿¡]` plus a closed stoplist of unambiguous Spanish words).
4. **Backend guard** (`tests/architecture/test_app_text.py`, S2).
   - It walks the AST of every string constant and f-string part in `app/**` and the operator scripts, skipping docstrings, and applies the same marker list.
   - Exempt paths are a closed list, each with a reason and a test that fails when an entry matches nothing: `app/migrations/**`, `spreadsheet_import/adapters/layout.py`, `cells.py`, `domain/rows.py`, `catalog/adapters/import_port.py`.
   - It also pins the changed constants as different from their old Spanish values.

All four guards parse files only, so they are date-independent and fast. Each is shown failing on a fixture: a renamed key keeping "Guardar", a function entry, a Spanish message in Python, a literal in a ternary or a variable. Each is shown passing on a sheet word and on a format.

### Terminology

- S1b-2a writes every `_In the app_` line of CONTEXT.md in English: the glossary headword (Balance, Credit, Payment, Stock withdrawal, …), the pricing-rule words, and proposed words where no headword exists (Home, More, Settings). Lines with "no word of its own" keep it.
- S1b-2a's reviewer approves the list against the headwords. From then on, no group edits CONTEXT.md.
- S7 turns on a pytest: each `_In the app_` word appears verbatim in some value under `web/src/copy/`.

### Order

S1a ∥ S2 → S1b-1 → S1b-2a → S1b-2b → S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5 ∥ S6 → S7.

Subtasks land on the task branch, and the task merges to `master` once, after S7. A half-English app never reaches a seller.

### Where new code coexists with old shapes

- **CODING_STANDARDS §10** still says "Every word on screen is Spanish". `app-text.md` (PM) governs this task, and S7 rewrites that bullet to "Every word on screen is English, the glossary's `_In the app_` word", so the standards stop contradicting the product.
- **Design material stays Spanish:** `pantallas/` and `muestra.md`, plus the muestra guard and the design-side `section` crop. Until S7, `screens.json`'s built-side steps keep the old words. S7 makes them English.
- **Stored businesses** keep their Spanish categories, header and footer (seller data).

### §7 and §8

Nothing is emitted. Nothing widens what an input reaches.

Costs:
- the guards: a constant read of `web/src` per test run;
- upkeep: unchanged, one edit per published pin at its next trigger or day change.

### Known risks

- An unaccented Spanish word that is not in the baseline and not on the stoplist passes; review owns it.
- `SAME_IN_BOTH` is a hole by design; each entry carries a reason a reviewer reads.
- Until S7, groups are held to the glossary by review alone.
- Longer English may clip; `copyLimits` counts characters and the S7 captures are judged by eye.
- Plurals ("1 customers") are covered only by the `SAMPLES` render and review.

### Subtasks

`### Subtasks` written by the system from the structured plan: no exact `### Subtasks` line (outside code fences) was found in this section, so the block below was built from the structured plan. Any list the Tech Lead wrote under another heading is not the one the Developers read.

#### S1a: move every front text into web/src/copy/ with no wording change

Create the keyed text package `web/src/copy/`, a pure, mechanical move. No visible text changes.

**What to build**
- `web/src/copy/index.ts` re-exports one section file per area. Every section exists from the start, even if empty: `common`, `auth`, `settings`, `sales`, `customers`, `varieties`, `stock`, `catalog`, `home`, `analysis`, `expenses`, `onboarding`, `refusals`, `format`, `shell`, `states`. Later subtasks never edit `index.ts`.
- Move the 16 copy modules (`*Messages.ts` and `messages.ts` under `web/src/screens`, `components/estadosMessages.ts`, `sales/messages.ts`) into their sections.
  - Keep each section's `LIMITED_COPY` and `LONG_REFUSALS`.
  - Update `web/src/copy/copyLimits.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_MODULES` and its glob so they read the sections.
- Move the texts of `web/src/api/refusals.ts` into `copy/refusals.ts`. `refusals.ts` keeps `Record<RefusalCode, Refusal>`, exhaustive, and reads the text from there.
- **Harvest `common`.** Every value that appears in two or more of today's copy modules or `refusals.ts` moves to `common`, still in Spanish, and the others import it. `messageSources.test.ts` keeps passing, with its owners updated.
- Type every entry `as const`. A parametrised entry stays a function.
- **Generate the baseline.** `web/src/architecture/fixtures/copyBaseline.es.json` maps each key path to its Spanish value. A function entry is stored as its output rendered with `SAMPLES`. Write the small script that generates it under `web/scripts/`, run it once, and commit the output. The file is never hand-edited.

**Not in scope:** literals still inline in screens (S1b-1 ledgers them); any wording.

**Acceptance**
- `tsc`, `oxlint`, vitest and e2e pass, with no test edited except import paths and `copyLimits.test.ts`'s module list.
- `git grep` finds no `*Messages.ts` outside `copy/`.
- The baseline JSON lists every moved value.
- A screen-rendering test still shows the same Spanish text.

Seams: `web/src/copy/copyLimits.test.ts (copyProblems over each section)`, `web/src/architecture/messageSources.test.ts (findMessageCopies)`, `existing screen tests through Testing Library, unchanged expectations`.

Depends on: nothing.

#### S2: English seeds and catalog text, backend text guard, upkeep test, sample keeps its data

**Backend changes**
- **Seeds.** `app/expenses/domain/category.py`'s `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_NAMES` becomes ("Growing supplies", "Packaging", "Shipping and travel", "Other"). `app/catalog/domain/header_footer.py` gets the header "🌿 **CATALOG** 🌿" and the footer "📩 Questions and orders by private message.".
  - Migrations 0003 and 0006 keep their own copies, untouched.
  - No stored row is changed.
- **Catalog text.** In `app/catalog/domain/text.py`, the fixed words of a block become English: `• N% indica / M% sativa`, `• Aroma/flavour: <aroma>`, `• Price per gram: $X`, `• From N g: $Y each`. `NOTHING_TO_OFFER` becomes "Nothing to offer for now.".
  - `_money` and `_grams` stay byte-identical.
  - Update the catalog reference-set test one for one.
  - The front preview (`telegramPreview.cases.json`) follows: update its cases one for one.
- **Import.** In `app/spreadsheet_import/domain/plan.py`, the inline `"Catálogo"` and `"Categoría"` become named constants in `domain/rows.py`. Behaviour and accepted sheet words are unchanged.
- **Backend guard: `tests/architecture/test_app_text.py`.**
  - It walks the AST of every string constant and f-string literal part in `app/**/*.py` and `scripts/{drill,backup_copy,newest_backup}.py`, skipping docstrings.
  - It flags any hit of the marker list.
  - Exempt paths are closed: `app/migrations/**`, `app/spreadsheet_import/adapters/layout.py`, `cells.py`, `app/spreadsheet_import/domain/rows.py` and `app/catalog/adapters/import_port.py`. Each has a reason, and a test fails if an entry matches nothing.
  - Pinned constants: each changed constant differs from its old Spanish value.
  - It is shown failing on a fixture with a Spanish message, and passing on a sheet word.
- **The marker list.** Create `web/src/architecture/spanishMarkers.json`, which holds `{"characters": "áéíóúñü¿¡", "words": [...]}`. Its stoplist holds unambiguous Spanish function and verb words: de, del, la, las, los, el, que, para, por, con, sin, una, hoy, desde, tu, vos, ya, todavía, acá, borrar, guardá, cargá, …
  - It must not hit the English seeds, "Packaging", or the catalog words.
  - It is the only file under `web/` this subtask owns.
- **Upkeep test.** A test through `push(..., "upkeep")` with the fake Telegram:
  - a pin whose `last_published_text` is the old Spanish render gets exactly one edit and no post;
  - a second push sends nothing;
  - a pin with `last_failure` set is retried;
  - an English render over the limit takes the existing `TooLong` path while the triggering save succeeds.

**Design sample:** `scripts/design/sample_app.py` sets the sample business's categories, header and footer through the API to what `pantallas/` draws. These are seller data. `muestra.md` stays byte-identical, and `tests/design/test_muestra_seed.py` passes unedited.

**Acceptance:** pytest, mypy and ruff are green. A new business gets the English defaults (API test). The import still matches and adds categories by name. `contract.json` and migrations have no diff.

Depends on: nothing.

#### S1b-1: front text guards, ledgers, PENDING markers and the locale ban, with ADR-0048

Add the front guards. No wording changes in this subtask.

**1. `web/src/architecture/appText.ts`** exports `findVisibleLiterals(path, source)`, built on `ts.createSourceFile` from the installed `typescript` package.
- **Scope:** every non-test `.ts`/`.tsx` under `web/src`, except `copy/`, `architecture/`, `test/`, `design/` and `routes.ts`.
- **It flags** any string literal, no-substitution template, template head, middle or tail part, or JSX text that contains a Unicode letter.
- **Closed list of allowed contexts:**
  - import or export specifiers and dynamic import paths;
  - type positions;
  - operands of `===`/`!==` and `case` clauses;
  - property names and element-access arguments;
  - the `className` attribute and the class helper's arguments;
  - `data-*` attributes;
  - the props `type`, `role`, `id`, `name`, `htmlFor`, `inputMode`, `enterKeyHint`, `autoComplete`, `variant`, `action`, `icon`, `nombre`, `to`, `href`, `textOnly`, `kind`;
  - the first argument of `Intl.NumberFormat`.

  A context added later is added to this list with its reason, never as a per-file exemption.
- **Tests.** Fixtures as `.txt` files read with `?raw` cover a variable, a ternary, a template, a helper return, `document.title` and `window.confirm`, and each must be flagged. Comparisons and class names must pass.

**2. Ledgers.**
- `web/src/architecture/textLeft/<owner>.json`, one per owner: `shared` (S1b-2b), `getting-in-settings`, `sales-customers`, `varieties-stock-catalog`, `home-analysis-expenses-onboarding`.
- Assign every offending file to exactly one owner. Components, `shell/`, `flash/`, `format/`, `api/`, `domain/`, `stock/` and `sales/` go to `shared`.
- `appText.real.test.ts` fails on any offending file not in a ledger, and on any ledger entry that no longer offends (shrink-only).
- A pytest (`tests/test_text_ledgers.py`) asserts the ledgers are pairwise disjoint.

**3. Spanish rule over `copy/`** (`copySpanish.ts` plus a test).
- It skips any section exporting `PENDING = true`.
- Otherwise, each value is rendered with `SAMPLES` for functions, then NFC-normalised, trimmed and case-folded. It fails when either:
  - it equals any value in `copyBaseline.es.json` (keys ignored), unless that section's exported `SAME_IN_BOTH: readonly {value, reason}[]` lists it;
  - it hits `spanishMarkers.json`, read through Vite (the file lives under `web/`).
- **Fixtures:**
  - a renamed key keeping "Guardar" fails;
  - a function entry rendering a baseline value fails;
  - an accented new string fails;
  - an English string passes.

**4. PENDING.** Add `export const PENDING = true` to every section.

**5. Locale ban.** A test fails on `toLocale` or `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/`Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` in any non-test file under `web/src`.

**6. ADR-0048.** Record the package, the two front guards, the backend guard (S2), the ledgers and `PENDING` (both removed in S7), and the known risks. Record also that `pantallas/` and `muestra.md` stay Spanish design reference: `section` crops run on the design side only.

**Acceptance:** vitest is green on the real tree, with every current offender in a ledger. Each guard is shown failing on its fixtures.

Seams: `web/src/architecture/appText.ts::findVisibleLiterals (fixture tests + real-tree test)`, `web/src/architecture/copySpanish.ts (fixture tests + real-tree test)`, `tests/test_text_ledgers.py`.

Depends on: `copy-move`, `backend-text`.

#### S7: remove the ratchet, turn on the term check, docs, standards, routes check and design evidence

**Remove the ratchet**
- Assert that no section exports `PENDING`, then delete the mechanism and its handling in `copySpanish.ts`. The baseline comparison and the marker rule then apply to every section.
- Assert every `textLeft/*.json` is empty, then delete the directory, its loading and `tests/test_text_ledgers.py`. The visible-literal rule then has no allowance at all.

**Turn on the term check:** a pytest asserting each `_In the app_` word of CONTEXT.md appears verbatim in some value under `web/src/copy/`. It reads the TS sources as text.

**Routes check:** a test that no path segment in `web/src/routes.ts` or `scripts/design/screens.json` equals a segment of any `old` path in `app/legacy_front_paths.json`, and that every path is ASCII. The redirect table is untouched.

**Docs**
- BRIEF.md: "Interface in English by default; code and docs in English".
- `docs/product/design-system/README.md`: English is the app's language, and `pantallas/` is the reference for structure, not wording.
- `app-text.md`: drop its precedence note once every capability doc is updated.
- CODING_STANDARDS §10's first bullet becomes "Every word on screen is English, using the glossary's `_In the app_` word". The rest stays.
- Amend ADR-0048 with the removal.

**Design tooling**
- The built-side steps of `scripts/design/screens.json` (`click`, `press` and `wait` texts that target the built app) become the English the app shows. Design-side `section` labels stay Spanish.
- Seams: `web/src/design/screens.test.ts` and `routes.test.ts` only.

**Evidence**
- One whole-registry `comparisons.mjs --strict` run, exit 0, judged by eye on phone and computer, both themes.
- List every clip found at 320 px or 130 % text and fix it CSS-only with existing tokens, or file it.
- Record the fontsource version.

**PR lists:** every edited test across the task, every shortened text, and every `SAME_IN_BOTH` entry with its reason.

Seams: `web/src/architecture/copySpanish.ts and appText.real.test.ts (no allowance)`, `tests/test_app_text_terms.py (CONTEXT.md terms vs copy)`, `tests/test_routes_english.py`, `web/src/design/screens.test.ts`, `web/src/routes.test.ts`.

Depends on: `screens-getting-in-settings`, `screens-sales-customers`, `screens-varieties-stock-catalog`, `screens-home-analysis-expenses-onboarding`.
